- 2026-11-04: step 4.2 — CSV parsing + column inference + field suggestions as a pure-stdlib library `backend/internal/csvparse` (no DB, no routes, no deps; 4.5's mapping endpoint consumes `Parse` + `Suggest`). **Surface:** `Field`/`Fields` (date_executed, date_posted, amount, description, category, running_balance — plan 4.2's vocabulary; running_balance has no 0002 column: it exists so the mapping UI can show it and the import step can sanity-check it), `ColumnKind` (unknown|text|date|amount + `String()`), `Column` (index, heading, kind, date format, running-balance flag), `File` (delimiter, encoding label, has header, header, data rows, columns), `Suggest` → ranked `ScoredColumn`s per field in `Fields` order (zero-score columns omitted; score desc, then index asc), `ErrEmpty` (no data rows — **header-only files included**: a header with zero rows has nothing to import). **Decode:** UTF-8 BOM stripped (label utf-8); `FF FE`/`FE FF` → `decodeUTF16` — stdlib `utf16.Decode` takes `[]uint16` (no byte-level API, and `utf16.LittleEndian`/`BigEndian` do not exist), so the bytes are walked manually into units first; a bad unit surfaces as U+FFFD rather than an error (viewer-grade: never lose the file); valid UTF-8 passes through; else **latin-1: every byte becomes the same-value rune, re-encoded UTF-8** (raw passthrough would leave an invalid-UTF-8 Go string that breaks the downstream JSON/Postgres layers). **Delimiter sniff:** first five non-empty lines, candidates `,`/`;`/tab, counts via a **quote-aware `countColumns`** (naive `strings.Count` mis-snips RBC-style `"Grocery Store, Ltd."` — escaped `""` pairs skipped, stray quote degrades to best-effort); **a candidate that splits no line is fallback-only** — a non-delimiter looks "perfectly consistent" (one column per line) and steals ragged files from the real delimiter; among splitting candidates: fewest distinct column counts → larger max count → fixed priority. **Header detection:** the first row is DATA when its first non-empty cell parses as a date, or it carries ≥1 date cell AND ≥1 amount cell (data-first layouts); otherwise it is the trimmed header. **Inference (sampleCap = 500 rows):** per cell, date is tried before amount and the two are **disjoint** (a date cell never counts as an amount); kind = date or amount when more than half the non-empty cells parse, else text (unknown when no non-empty cells). `ParseAmount` (exported — 4.6's importer reuses it): parens → then a **loop stripping `c$`/`$`/leading-`-`/trailing-`-` in ANY order** (a fixed prefix order failed on `-$85.40`, common in exports) → body is a bare int, `\d+\.\d{1,4}`, or US-grouped `\d{1,3}(,\d{3})+(\.\d{1,4})?`. `classifyDateFormat`: any part > 12 is decisive (first part → dd-mm-yyyy, second → mm-dd-yyyy), else an ISO plurality, else the US-convention default mm-dd-yyyy. **Running balance:** over ordered pairs of amount columns, row-aligned values must satisfy `b - bPrev = ±a` within 0.005 (2-decimal tolerance), needing ≥3 matched pairs AND one sign dominant (≥70%) so a coincidental fit cannot pass. **Scoring:** kind baseline (date 3.0 posted / 2.0 executed; amount 3.0 — 1.0 if flagged running-balance; running_balance 4.0 if flagged / 0.5 for plain amount columns; text 3.0 description / 1.0 category) + case-insensitive heading substring boosts (post/date, execut/transaction/value, amount/debit/credit, balance/running/solde, description/desc/payee/memo/details/narrative/particulars, categor/merchant/code/mcc) + position (first date column / first text column +0.5). Tests: table-driven `ParseAmount`/`parseDateCell`, date-format classification (decisive part / plurality / default), running-balance consistent/inverted/random/too-short, parse-level (encodings built as raw `[]byte` — BOM/UTF-16LE/BE/latin-1 0xE9→é, delimiter sniffing, quoting incl. escaped `""` and a stray apostrophe, headerless + data-first + header-only `ErrEmpty`, ragged + trailing-empty), and synthetic RBC/TD/Scotia/CIBC/BMO/Desjardins-style fixtures with top-pick assertions (style samples only — the real per-bank headers/formats + source URLs are captured in 4.4, not claimed here).
- 2026-11-04: step 4.3 — OFX/QFX parser as a pure-stdlib library `backend/internal/ofxparse` (no DB, no routes, no deps; 4.6's importer consumes it exactly like csvparse). **Surface:** `Transaction{DatePosted, Amount, Name, RefNum}` — all fields are RAW tag strings (missing tag -> ""; 4.6 decides what is fatal: an empty DatePosted/Amount becomes a row error there, not a parse error), `Parse(io.Reader) -> ([]Transaction, error)`, `ErrEmpty` (no non-empty STMTTRN record). **State machine** over trimmed lines (CRLF-safe, BOM stripped): `<STMTTRN` (EqualFold — note: spec OFX openers have NO closing `>`) starts a record and flushes any prior unterminated one; a lone `^` or `</STMTTRN` flushes; tags are matched case-insensitively after stripping the leading `<`: `dtposted`, `trnamt`, `name|payee`, `refnum|checknum`; envelope junk (Signon, ACCTINQ, CLOSINGBATCH...), section tags and unknown record tags (FITID) are skipped; the final unterminated record is kept. **Both real shapes are unified:** QFX (the BMO/RBC export) uses the SAME `<STMTTRN` line for the wrapping section (DTSTART/DTEND/CURRCODE/BALANCE) AND each `^`-closed record; spec OFX wraps records in `<BANKTRANLIST` with `</STMTTRN`-closed records — the **empty-record drop rule** (all four fields "") doubles as the section/record disambiguator (the QFX section opener shows up as exactly such a record; the ACCTINQ `STMTTRN:` request tag is a colon-tag, never an opener). DTPOSTED time + tz suffix (`20251005123000[-5]`) stays raw. **unescape:** backslash before `~ , : ; ^` -> literal, `~Uhhhh` -> four-hex rune (Desjardins-style accented names — the bounds check `i+6 <= len(s)` must come BEFORE the `s[i+2:i+6]` slice; a truncated `~U00E` panicked before the fix), `~N` -> newline, unknown sequences verbatim. Viewer-grade: a partial record is returned, never an error. Tests: full BMO-style QFX envelope (the ACCTINQ request tag + the section record must not leak; BOM variant), spec-OFX `<BANKTRANLIST` fixture (PAYEE alias, `~U00E9`, tz-suffixed DTPOSTED, FITID ignored), unescape table (21 cases incl. truncated/bad hex), `ErrEmpty` table (empty / blank / envelope-only / ACCTINQ / section-only / unknown-tags-only record), partial records, CRLF + last record cut off (both kept, file order).
- 2026-11-04: step 4.4 — spec-driven JSON parser + the builtin Canadian-FI import presets + the verified-format record. **jsonparse (new package):** `Parse(io.Reader, Spec) -> (*File, error)`, `ErrEmpty` (no array found, or an array with no object items) — the returned `File` mirrors csvparse.File (raw-string rows in the canonical `Fields` order) so 4.6 imports JSON exactly like CSV. `Spec{ArrayPointers []string, Fields []FieldSpec{Name, Pointers}}` + `Valid()` (pointers non-empty, names unique, at least one field) — the spec IS the JSON document `import_schemas.spec` stores, so it is validated before persistence, not after. **RFC 6901** `resolve` + `unescapeRef`: `~1` -> `/` must be applied BEFORE `~0` -> `~` (the reverse order corrupts a literal `~1`); array indices resolve against `[]any`. `dec.UseNumber()` — **json.Number keeps the raw numeric literal** (float64 would silently corrupt 20-digit values; money must never round-trip through binary floats); numbers stay strings in the rows. First scalar wins per field: the first pointer that resolves to a string/number/bool is taken; non-object array items are skipped (a bare string array is a transaction list); trailing data after the JSON document is ignored (exporters pad). **presets (new package):** builtins are CODE, not DB rows — `presets.All()` seeds the 4.5 import page; "save mapping as preset" writes per-user copies into `import_schemas` (0001, `builtin = false`). `Format` (csv|ofx|json) equals `import_schemas.kind` verbatim. 8 builtins in canonical order: generic-csv (no Map — 4.2's Suggest produces the pre-fill), generic-json (array pointers "", /transactions, /data, /items, /records, /txns + alias-pointer map), ofx-generic (no Map — 4.3's ofxparse already emits date_posted/amount/name/refnum, the mapping is identity), td, scotiabank, bmo, rbc, desjardins. **CIBC deliberately has no builtin:** its authoritative record is the PDF eStatement (7 years, no machine format) — `docs/IMPORT-PRESETS.md` documents the third-party-converter path instead of encoding a converter's output. **FieldRef zero-value trap (caught before the tests ran):** `Index`'s zero value is 0 = "column 0", so every builtin CSV ref writes `Index: -1` explicitly (no positional fallback); the doc comment says so. `CSVColumns(headers)`: per field, first matching candidate header (case-insensitive, trimmed) -> positional `Index` when >= 0 -> -1 (unmapped); headerless exports pass an empty row (the Desjardins business CSV is 14 positional fields with NO header row); nil for non-CSV and map-less presets (generic-csv). `Match(filename, headers)`: the extension picks the family — .csv: filename token (exact alphanumeric tokens; "std" never claims "td"), then header signature (ALL verified headers present, extras tolerated) -> generic-csv; .ofx/.qfx: token -> ofx-generic; .json -> generic-json; any other extension nil (4.5 asks the user). `JSONSpec()` emits the jsonparse.Spec in the canonical csvparse.Fields order. **docs/IMPORT-PRESETS.md (new):** per bank — exact format, how to obtain it, source URL(s), fetched 2026-11-04, and the drift note (re-verify every URL at release, plan 11.3): Scotiabank header row VERIFIED (Filter, Date, Description, Sub-description, Type of Transaction, Amount, Balance — official help article + fininja); TD CSV header row UNVERIFIED by design (no source publishes it — the preset claims by token only, aliases + Suggest fill); BMO/RBC/Desjardins OFX (QFX) from the web download menu; Desjardins business CSV headerless + 62-day/12-month windows + 7-year PDF relevés (FR/EN, `1 234,56`); CIBC PDF-centric with no preset. The 4.2/4.3 parser fixtures are flagged as synthetic style samples, not verified bank layouts. **Tests:** presets_test.go — TestBuiltinShape (8 IDs unique + canonical order, Get round-trip, CIBC absent, unknown nil, per-format invariants, JSONSpec -> spec.Valid()), TestMatch (21 cases), TestCSVColumns (Scotia verified row with case/padding noise, TD aliases, no-match all -1, a synthetic positional preset for the Index path: headerless / unmatched / matching-header-beats-Index, nil for OFX/JSON/generic-csv), TestJSONSpec, TestTokens, TestHitExactOnly; jsonparse_test.go covers resolve/unescapeRef ordering, UseNumber raw-literal preservation, first-scalar-wins, skipped non-object items, trailing data, ErrEmpty. **Verified:** Docker (golang:1.27.3) gofmt clean, go vet clean, `go test ./internal/jsonparse/ ./internal/presets/` green. No new deps (stdlib only). **§0 caps overage:** 6 files (5 new + this bullet) and 1273 insertions / 0 deletions (1273 diff lines) — over the ~500-line cap (within the 8-file cap); kept as ONE commit per the 1.3c/1.4a/2.5a/2.5b/2.6a/2.6b/3.1/3.2/3.3/4.1/4.2 precedent, overage also carried to `docs/ITERATION-1-REPORT.md`.

UserChip prompt = `$derived($user && $settings && onboarded_at === null)` — positive knowledge only (hidden while loading or when the load failed). Onboarding page rewritten: three states (`!$user` → sign-in link / done → "already done" + back link / form: email confirm/edit + currency + 3 notification checkboxes + read-only category chips); fields initialize once at mount from the store with the profile email claim as fallback (the same value the backend seeded at bootstrap); `envelopeMessage()` carves the 3.1 envelope out of `ApiError.message` (the api client folds the raw body in as "API 400: {…}" and has no `.body` property — `api/client.ts` deliberately untouched to hold the 8-file cap). `profileComplete()` deleted (claim-based gate; all 4 use sites replaced). i18n: `onboarding.placeholder` key replaced by the form's key set. **svelte-check/Svelte-5 typing gotchas hit + worked around:** a local `$derived` referenced inside a `$state(…)` initializer fires `state_referenced_locally`; `$state(fn)` is typed as the function type itself (not its call result); legacy `writable` stores have **no `.value`** accessor — the clean pattern is a plain helper + `$store` auto-subscription (lints 0/0). Verified: `frontend-fmt` / `frontend-lint` (svelte-check 0 errors 0 warnings + eslint + prettier) / `frontend-build` all green in the `node:26.13.0` image; live E2E against the rebuilt dev backend — GET `onboarded_at:null` → PATCH `{onboarded:true,…}` → 200 full Settings stamped → GET persists → PATCH `onboarded:false` → 400 `invalid_request` envelope (dev DB then reset to the pristine first-login state). **§0 caps: 8 files (7 code + this bullet) = exactly the file cap, ≈410 diff lines ≤ ~500** — within caps, no split.

Down drops the column only — the table stays; the migrate lifecycle is pinned accordingly (up -> 6 with the column present, re-up -> 6, one-step down -> 5 with the column gone). **One mapping-document shape serves jobs and presets:** `{"kind","array_pointers" (json only),"map":{<app field>:{headers,index} | {pointers}}}` is stored verbatim in BOTH `import_jobs.mapping` and `import_schemas.spec` — the 4.6 runner reads a job's mapping and applies a saved preset through the same code path. **413 is set-mapping only:** the 1 MiB raw-body cap (`maxMappingBytes`) fires before unmarshal and before the job fetch (the document is user-edited JSON; - 2026-11-05: step 4.6 — import runner + de-duplication (plan's "Job runner: normalize rows (dates/amounts), compute dedup hash, batched upsert; result {inserted, skipped, errors[]}"; core requirement: re-submit the same file inserts 0, an overlapping later file inserts only new rows). **One route:** `POST /v1/imports/{id}/run` (new `importrun.go`, 316 lines; no request body — the job's stored mapping + file drive everything). **Status walk** (0005 vocabulary): the job must be in `mapping` — anything else (uploaded/importing/done/failed) is a 409 `conflict` "import job must be in the mapping state to run" (terminal jobs are immutable, so a re-run is a 409); `StartRun` flips it to `importing`; the run ends `done` (result jsonb, error NULL) or `failed` (error text, result NULL) via `FinishRun`. **One mapped-row code path:** `mappedFile(m, file)` factored out of the 4.5 preview logic (importmap.go — parses, applies the mapping to ALL rows, returns names/rows/total/csvFile/msg); the preview is now `mappedFile` + first N (the pinned preview tests stay byte-green), the runner consumes every row — no drift between what the user previewed and what lands. **Normalization** (runner-side; the parsers hand back raw strings): CSV dates via the mapped column's csvparse-inferred format (mm-dd-yyyy / dd-mm-yyyy / iso8601 -> Go layouts) with a layout-list fallback; JSON tries [RFC3339, "2006-01-02", mm-dd, dd-mm]; OFX applies the zone fix then tries [pinned "20060102150405[-0700]", no-zone, date-only]; amounts via exported `repo.CanonicalAmount`; description trimmed ("" legal); `date_executed = date_posted` (the 0002 CHECK); category/notes/raw nil (phase 5 categorizes; the source bytes already sit in `import_jobs.file_encrypted`). **Result** `{"inserted":N,"skipped":M,"errors":[{"row":i,"field":...,"message":...}]}` — `row` is the 0-based index within the parsed data rows, `errors` is never nil (a clean run renders `[]`); a bad date/amount is NON-fatal (the row is reported, the rest of the file still imports); a missing mapping / unparseable file / empty file / unmapped essential field (date_posted or amount) is fatal -> `failed` + error text. **Dedup** is the untouched 2.6a key: the runner never computes the hash — it calls `repo.TransactionStore.Upsert` per row (`ON CONFLICT (account_id, date_posted, dedup_hash) DO NOTHING`, conflict -> `findByDedup` -> inserted=false) inside ONE db transaction, so the guarantees hold: same file re-submitted -> 0 inserted / N skipped; overlapping file -> only its new rows. **Repo** (mirrors the 4.5 SetMapping pattern): `StartRun` sets `importing` for an owned job — the status precondition is enforced at the API, not in the SQL (documented in the repo comment; nothing runs concurrently until the 5.4 worker); `FinishRun` validates the status against the 0005 vocabulary, then writes status+result+error (jsonb bound as a string — pgx rejects a `*json.RawMessage` bind, the same rule as SetMapping; nil = NULL). **Server ifaces:** `ImportStore` gains StartRun/FinishRun; `TransactionStore` gains `Upsert` (2.6a deliberately kept it off the API surface; the runner is its first caller). **Wire:** 200 with the raw job row (no envelope) for BOTH `done` and `failed` — the HTTP call succeeded, the outcome lives in `status`/`result`/`error`; 401/404/503 as pinned in 4.5 (malformed id -> 404 after requireUser; nil store -> 503 before). **Tests:** `TestImportRunGates` (mem: 401/404/503/409 + envelope), `TestImportRunMemory` (insert, terminal 409, same-file 0/3, row errors), `TestImportRun` (CORE — real testcontainer Postgres + real ImportStore/TransactionStore: re-import 0 inserted, overlap 1/2, row-level errors, failed run), and the repo `run finish round trip` subtest. **Verified:** `make backend-fmt` / `backend-lint` / `backend-test` all green in Docker. **§0 caps overage:** 9 files / 924 insertions (+68 deletions) vs the ~8 files / ~500-line cap — over the line cap; one commit per the 4.4/4.5 precedent; the overage is carried to `docs/ITERATION-1-REPORT.md`.

- 2026-11-05: step 5.1 — LLM provider abstraction (plan's "`internal/llm`: `Categorizer` interface; OpenAI-compatible provider (base URL + key + model) — one implementation covers self-hosted llama.cpp, OpenAI, Azure, any compatible endpoint; strict JSON response parsing; 1 retry with format-nudge on malformed output; per-call timeout"). **Fixed layering:** 5.1 = "get valid JSON back from an LLM" — the `Categorizer.Categorize(ctx, Request{System, User})` call takes RAW prompt strings and returns `Response{JSON json.RawMessage, Model, PromptTokens, CompletionTokens}`; the response SHAPE (5.2's `{i, category, confidence, rationale}` array) and the 0.75 threshold are 5.2's, and 5.2's golden tests mock THIS interface. No server wiring (5.4 wires the worker); nothing imports the package yet. **`New(baseURL, apiKey, model, opts...)`:** baseURL is the OpenAI-compatible root and MUST end in `/v1` (trailing slash normalized; the package comment pins this — 5.4's env default is the llama.cpp Service URL + `/v1`); `apiKey == ""` = self-hosted, no Authorization header sent; POST `{base}/chat/completions` with `temperature` pinned 0 (determinism) and `max_tokens` (option, default 1024, omitted ≤0). **No `response_format` in the request** — maximum compatibility across llama.cpp/OpenAI/other compatible servers; the nudge retry is the JSON guarantee, not a server feature. **Strict JSON** (`parse.go` `extractJSON`): trim → strip a ``` fence if present → full-body `json.RawMessage` validation → fallback substring from the first `{`/`[` to the last `}`/`]` → else malformed; a success is COMPACTED (re-marshalled) so downstream JSON is canonical. **Retry:** first attempt malformed → exactly ONE retry with the user message suffixed by a fixed nudge ("…not valid JSON. Respond with ONLY a single JSON value -- no prose, no markdown fences."); still malformed → `llm: malformed JSON response after 2 attempts: <200-char snippet>`; HTTP status / timeout / no-content errors are NOT retried (fail fast, 1 request). **Per-call timeout:** `WithTimeout` (default 60s) wraps EACH attempt in its own `context.WithTimeout` — per-call, not total, so a slow 4B CPU model gets 60s per attempt. `usage` prompt/completion tokens are captured (0 when the server omits them) — the seed for 5.4's token/cost tracking. **Tests:** 12, all `net/http/httptest` (no containers, no real network): happy path (asserts temperature 0, max_tokens, message roles, Bearer), keyless no-header, fenced JSON, prose-wrapped JSON, malformed-then-good (asserts exactly 2 requests + nudge present in the 2nd), malformed-twice (exactly 2, "after 2 attempts"), HTTP-500 no-retry, non-JSON body, empty choices ("no content"), per-attempt timeout (50ms vs 300ms sleep, completes <2s), URL construction (trailing-slash variants hit `/v1/chat/completions`), ctx cancellation. **Verified:** `make backend-fmt` / `backend-lint` / `backend-test` all green in Docker (`internal/llm` 0.4s). **§0 caps overage:** 3 files / 673 insertions vs the ~8 files / ~500-line cap — over the line cap (mostly test matrix); one commit per the 4.4/4.5/4.6 precedent; the overage is carried to `docs/ITERATION-1-REPORT.md`.

this package never touches crypto. **Categories have no description column** — "numbered descriptions" = the numbered TRANSACTION descriptions in the user prompt. **Pinned formats** (`prompt.go`, exported so the golden tests pin byte-exact strings): `SystemPrompt` = role line + "Assign each numbered transaction to exactly one of the categories below." + numbered category names + rules (choose only from the list; confidence calibrated in [0,1], 1.0 only when unambiguous; closest-category-with-low-confidence when nothing fits) + "Respond with ONLY a JSON array" with the `{i, category, confidence, rationale}` example; `UserPrompt` = "Transactions:" + one 1-based line per item, `i. <description|"(no description)">` with ` (amount: <canonical>)` appended when the amount is non-empty. **`Run(ctx, c, categories, items) (map[int]Suggestion, error)`:** fail fast before any provider call (`no categories provided` / `no items to categorise` — pinned: 0 provider calls); One bad entry NEVER fails the batch — it is skipped and that item simply has no suggestion (5.4 then marks it review); duplicate `i` -> FIRST entry wins; provider errors propagate unchanged. **Policy:** `DefaultThreshold = 0.75`; `Suggestion.NeedsReview(t)` is STRICTLY below — exactly 0.75 is accepted (pinned by `TestRunConfidenceBoundary`). **Tests:** 12 golden tests, ALL via a mock `llm.Categorizer` (no HTTP, no containers): happy path (IDs resolved, canonical names), confidence boundary, unknown category skipped, missing entry (absent map key), clamping, duplicate first-wins, out-of-range i, case-insensitive match, provider error propagation, empty-input fail-fast subtests, `TestPrompts` (full pinned-string equality for both prompts incl. `(no description)`/amount variants), malformed-JSON defense (non-array + object). **Verified:** `make backend-fmt` / `backend-lint` / `backend-test` all green in Docker (`categorize` 0.005s). Within the §0 caps (3 files / 493 insertions) — no overage note.

- 2026-11-09: UI feedback round 1 — appearance theme + settings-page completeness (user feedback: "we should have a dark mode/light mode setting, with the default set to respect prefers-color-scheme"; the sign-in default currency and category management were absent from Settings). **Theme model:** the server is the single source of truth — `0011_users_theme.sql` adds `users.theme text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'system' CHECK (theme IN ('light','dark','system'))`; `Settings`/`SettingsPatch` carry it (PATCH validated by `validThemes` in `validatePatch` → 400 `theme must be one of light|dark|system`); GET/PATCH `/api/v1/settings` round-trip verified live. **Frontend application:** Tailwind v4.3.3 class-based dark mode via `@custom-variant dark (&:where(.dark, .dark *))` in `app.css` (the v4 default is media-based; class-based is required so a single stored setting drives the whole app); `html { color-scheme: light } html.dark { color-scheme: dark }` so native control internals (select popups, scrollbars) follow the page, not the OS. **FOUC:** a pre-paint IIFE in `app.html` (before `%sveltekit.head%`) reads `localStorage.ftrk-theme` (fallback `'system'` → `prefers-color-scheme`) and toggles `document.documentElement.classList('dark')` before first paint — a dark-mode user never sees a light flash; try/catch keeps private mode working (theme still applies, just not pre-paint-cached). The `+layout.svelte` `$effect` on `$settings?.theme` re-applies the authoritative server value on load and on every change, mirrors it to `ftrk-theme` for the next pre-paint, and — while the setting is `'system'` — subscribes `matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)')` with a cleanup on re-run so the OS preference is followed live. **Palette audit:** one global lookaround perl pass over all 17 `frontend/src/**/*.svelte` appended the dark twin to every light utility (390 pairs; verified: every `dark:` token immediately follows its light twin, zero strays, no double insertions; no dynamic class construction exists in the codebase, so a static pass is complete). Untouched by design: `bg-zinc-900`/`bg-zinc-700` (already-dark accents), `bg-red-500`/`bg-green-600` (saturated buttons), `text-white`, `text-zinc-400`. **Settings page:** new "Preferences" card (theme select `system|light|dark` + currency select from the curated `CURRENCIES`, with a stored non-curated ISO code kept as an extra option so it can't be lost; both PATCH on change and apply live — the layout effect reacts to the store update) and a "Categories" card (list with an `active` checkbox — `active` is what the manual-entry/review pickers offer — inline rename with explicit Save/Cancel + Enter/Escape (deliberately no commit-on-blur: a blur→click race would commit after Cancel), add, and delete behind `confirm()`; delete is safe because `transactions.category_id` is `ON DELETE SET NULL` — its transactions become uncategorized). New client fns in `lib/api/categories.ts`: `createCategory` (POST 201), `patchCategory` (PATCH 200; `parent_id` tri-state via `JSON.stringify` omitting `undefined`), `deleteCategory` (DELETE 204). i18n: 18 new `settings.*` keys in `en.json` (+ intro rewrite). **Verified live:** migration applied (goose 11; dev-DB surgery note — `goose_db_version` has surrogate `id` AND `version_id`; an earlier `up -d --build backend` never re-ran the one-shot `migrate` service, so 0011 had to be applied via `up -d migrate`); settings GET/PATCH round-trip incl. 400 on `theme:"sepia"`; category create/rename/409-duplicate/active-toggle/delete/204 all green. Frontend `vite build` clean; pre-paint script + `color-scheme` confirmed in `build/index.html`.

- 2026-11-09: UI feedback round 2 — Explore defaults + spending/income filter + metric-into-the-Type-filter (user feedback: the Explore page opened on an empty view with the test data, because it is all negative "spending"; "we are tracking spending … when we're showing spending, we should be converting them to positive values to show 'you spent x dollars on this thing'"; "a setting to let people filter for spending (presumably negative values), income (positive values)"; then: "i think the metric (spend/income/net) should go into the filter vs the view tab. that allows us to also have the min/max amount filters be absolute values in 2/3 cases, so if users are doing spend, they don't have to put -10 and -100 if they want to look at small expenditures"). **Defaults (supersede the 6.4 pins):** `chart` default table → **pie**, `group` default month → **category** (the user's asks). **The metric is no longer a URL field:** the `metric` select is removed from the view bar (which shrinks to chart kind / grouping / Save-as-card), and the **Type filter (`sign`) IS the metric** — spending → the `spend` metric, income → `income`, all → `net`, derived client-side by `metricForSign(sign)` (state.ts) and used by the ExploreView for chart values and the pie exclusion (non-positive groups hidden, cap note unchanged). `ExploreState` drops the `metric` field entirely (the `METRICS` const, the parse pick, and the serialize step are gone); the table still renders all three columns. **Absolute min/max (the 2/3 cases):** with `sign` set, `min_amount`/`max_amount` are ABSOLUTE magnitudes — spending rows (amount < 0) match `-max..-min`, income rows (amount > 0) match `min..max`; with no sign the bounds stay raw signed values (unchanged). The server 400s negative bounds with a sign set: `min_amount must be non-negative when sign is set` / `max_amount must be non-negative when sign is set` (server/transactions.go, checked right after sign parse). Backend: `TransactionFilter` doc + the `List()` bound building (repo/transactions.go), the server 400s, the memTxStore mirror (spending skips c>=0 then flips to magnitude), the absolute-bounds subtest (replacing the old `sign=spending&min_amount=-10` case) + two new 400 cases, and four repo case-table entries. **Frontend URL state:** parseState maps a legacy `metric=` param onto `sign` — only when no explicit `sign` is present (an explicit sign always wins; spend→spending, income→income, net/invalid values dropped) — and strips a leading '-' from min/max whenever the resolved sign is `spending`; serializeState does the same strip, so `parseState(serializeState(s))` stays deep-equal and a shared `min=-10&sign=spending` URL normalizes to `min=10`. Dashboard cards saved under the old codec (with `metric=`) parse to the right sign automatically — no card migration. Canonical serialize order: `from,to,account,category,min,max,q,sign,chart,group`. The min/max labels switch with the Type (new i18n keys `min_spend_label`/`max_spend_label`/`min_income_label`/`max_income_label`; the four `metric_*` keys are deleted). **Live verification (dev DB: 22 tx, all negative, all |amount| > 10):** `sign=spending` → 200, 22 rows; `sign=spending&min_amount=5` → 22; `sign=spending&min_amount=3&max_amount=10` → 0; `sign=income` → 0; `min_amount=-100&max_amount=-50` (no sign) → 200, 7 rows; `sign=spending&min_amount=-10` → 400 with the new message. **Flagged consequence:** with Type=All (the default) on all-negative data the derived metric is net, so the default pie renders empty — the round-2 complaint re-appears under "All"; Type=Spending shows the spend pie. A consequence of the user-requested design, not a bug.

- 2026-11-09: UI feedback round 3 — default view spending + remembered "last settings" + pie legend table + dark-mode labels (user feedback: "the default view should still be spending, right now it was empty"; "once the user explores the page, it should default to the 'last settings'"; "the pie chart should show a legend with the values for the categories in a table beside the chart, so numbers and a visual are both present"; "the pie labels currently, in dark mode at least, are hard to read, since the white outlines make the text fuzzy"). **Default = spending:** the default `sign` is now 'spending' (state.ts parseState init + the page's resetFilters) — the round-2 flagged consequence (Type=All default → net metric → an empty pie on the all-negative dev data) is resolved. **Last settings:** a bare /explore reopens the user's last view — the page persists the full canonical state string to the localStorage key `ftrk-explore-state` (the URL-sync effect rewrites it on every change, incl. the first run); init precedence = URL params (any present) > remembered > defaults, so a shared/explicit link is deterministic and NEVER merges with remembered settings; Reset restores the defaults, which serialize to '' and are rewritten by the same effect (no explicit deleteItem); storage read/write failures (private mode) are silent no-ops. **The sign grammar:** the URL `sign` param is now `spending | income | all` — 'all' maps to the all view (''), the default 'spending' is omitted when serializing, the all view is the explicit `sign=all` token; legacy `metric=` URLs (pre-round-2) map onto the sign ONLY when the sign param is absent — spend → spending, income → income, net → '' (PRESERVED as the all view, since the default is now spending, not all); a legacy URL with no sign and no metric now parses to the default (spending), and its min/max bounds now get the absolute-magnitude normalization (min=-10 → min=10 with sign=spending — accepted consequence, noted here). **Pie legend table:** the pie's outside labels + label lines are OFF (the default white text outlines are unreadable in dark mode) and the chart pairs with a 2-column legend table (group swatch + label | the metric value, a formatCents of the integer-cents aggregate — rendered-numbers contract intact) listing exactly the rendered slices (the metric-positive groups) in slice order; the slice colors are pinned to ECharts 5's default 9-color palette in a `PIE_COLORS` const (ExploreView) so the swatches can't drift; the pie_excluded note and the hover tooltip stay. **Card hrefs:** the dashboard cards' click-through href now RE-SERIALIZES the stored state via a new `serializeStateForLink` (always-explicit sign — even the default `sign=spending`) through `resolve('/explore?…')`; never a bare /explore, since a bare URL opens the REMEMBERED last view, which may differ from the card's; stored all-view cards now hold `sign=all` (server cap 1024 — negligible growth). Codec: parseState(serializeState(s)) deep-equal holds for all three sign values; serializeState omits the default sign and emits sign=all for the all view.

- 2026-11-09: UI feedback round 4 — account management (user feedback: "i don't see anywhere one can define/create/manage accounts, on this page, or in settings" — the imports page forces an account pick with only the test-data account present; build account management in settings, linked from anywhere an account is picked). **Backend: ZERO work** — the step-3.1 CRUD exists and is tested; the client's wire shape was re-verified live this round (POST without `subtype` → 201 with `subtype:null`, i.e. JSON-omitted; PATCH `{"subtype":"chequings"}` → 200 with the updated row in the body; PATCH `{"subtype":null}` → 200 clear; DELETE → 204; list count restored). **Contract pinned:** types `bank|cash|credit_card|mortgage|investment|other`; name ≤ 255 runes; subtype ≤ 64 with tri-state PATCH semantics (absent = untouched, `null` = clear, string = set) and empty patch → 400; currency `^[A-Z]{3}$` REQUIRED on POST (no server default); duplicate name → 409; unknown id → 404; DELETE 204 and **cascades** (transactions 0002, import_jobs 0005, mortgages 0009) vs categories' SET NULL; there is NO GET-by-id (list only — the client never calls one). **Client** `frontend/src/lib/api/accounts.ts` rewritten in the categories.ts style: `Account`, `ACCOUNT_TYPES` (6), `AccountType`, `listAccounts`, `createAccount({name,type,currency,subtype?})` (subtype `undefined` when empty — `JSON.stringify` drops it), `AccountPatch` (tri-state subtype), `patchAccount`, `deleteAccount`. **Settings card** `<div id="accounts">` after the categories card in `settings/+page.svelte`, same loading/error/list/add-row structure: per-op state (add/edit/delete); a `$effect` prefills the new-account currency from the stored `settings.default_currency` while it is still `''` (the select has no empty option, so a user's choice sticks; resetting to `''` after add re-triggers the prefill); `accountCurrencyOptions(extra?)` keeps a currency outside the curated 35-code list selectable as an extra option (the prefs-picker rule — the backend accepts any ISO-4217 code); edit row = name (maxlength 255) + type/currency selects + subtype (maxlength 64) + Save/Cancel reusing the existing `settings.save`/`settings.cancel` keys, Enter commits / Escape cancels on both text inputs; display row = name + muted `(type label · currency · subtype)`; delete confirms via `confirm()` warning that the account's transactions, import jobs, and mortgages are removed too (the CASCADE); `default_schema_id` is deliberately NOT exposed (import-job-specific, stays null). **Picker links:** new shared key `common.manage_accounts` = "Manage in settings →" in a new top-level `common` group (after `app`); each link is `<a href={resolve('/settings#accounts')}>` — D-009 internal `<a>` with `resolve()` from `$app/paths`, imported on line 2 of each file (house convention); added to all 5 surfaces that pick an account: imports (3rd flex item in the account row), ManualEntry (account cell wrapped in a div, link under the label so a label click can't forward to the link), home (3rd flex item in the controls row), explore (under the account multi-select), mortgage (two spots: inline in the no-mortgage empty state + under the add form's account cell). Deliberately NOT linked: review/ (display-name map only), catalogs.ts, AppShell (nav poll). **en.json:** the `common` group, 17 `settings` keys after `cat_adding` (`accounts_title`/`accounts_note`, `acct_*`), `acct_type` as a NESTED object (svelte-i18n dotted-path resolution requires the object form), and both `no_accounts` hints (imports + manual_entry, identical lines) retargeted to "No accounts yet — add one in settings." **Verify:** svelte-check 0 errors / 0 warnings, vite build clean, live probe as above.

- 2026-11-10: dashboards rework (round 6) — multi-dashboard browse area + 12-col widget editor (user feedback: "dashboards should be a place to browse dashboards, not little 'cards'. dashboards should be a place where one can add tables, graphs, etc. each with their own saved queries, resize them, move them around, add custom text items in markdown, etc. you could consider a single dashboard to be comprised of cards, if cards could be made that flexible. but the dashboards tab should allow one to create+manage many dashboards, not just one."). The two reported card bugs (aria-label rendered as visible card text; save-as-dashboard-card wording) were fixed + committed first (9b8b59c); the rework was then planned in `docs/DASHBOARDS-REWORK.md` (the two-phase contract: P1 backend subagent → P2 frontend subagent → P3 parent re-verify + this bullet). **Model:** widget kinds v1 = `explore` (embeds the existing `ExploreView` — table + line/bar/pie; `content` = the canonical `explore_state` query string, "" = defaults = the widget's own saved query) + `markdown` (user text, sanitized render); layout = `grid grid-cols-1 gap-4 lg:grid-cols-12`, each widget `lg:col-span-N` with `span ∈ 1..12` (DB column, default 6; mobile renders every widget full-width); resize = per-widget size select (presets 3=¼ / 4=⅓ / 6=½ / 8=⅔ / 12=full); move = left/right buttons swapping `sort_order` with the neighbour (the same swap+PUT-order pattern the old cards page used; DOM order in the grid IS display order); drag-and-drop deliberately out (new dependency + a11y + testing cost). **P1 (d5b4eb1):** `0013_dashboards.sql` = `dashboards` (per-user, name) + `dashboard_widgets` (kind check, content, span check 1..12, sort_order, UNIQUE(dashboard_id, sort_order)); the SQL does NOT drop `dashboard_cards` — the ciphertext is DEK-encrypted at app layer, so the drop is owned by a one-time **app-level backfill** at server startup (after goose): every card → a per-user dashboard named "Default" as an `explore` widget (content = the card's explore_state, span 4 = the old 3-up grid, sort order preserved), idempotent no-op when the table is empty/absent, then the table is cleared + DROPPED (0008's down-migration becomes unreachable on post-backfill DBs; the house one-step-down test only covers from the tip — 0013's down drops the new tables). 9 routes: dashboards GET (with widget_count) / POST / PATCH(name) / DELETE + widgets GET / POST / PATCH / DELETE + `PUT /v1/dashboards/{id}/widgets/order` (204, bare id array; 400 "order must list every widget exactly once"); NO dashboard GET-by-id (clients resolve the row from the list); 400 messages verbatim ("span must be between 1 and 12", content caps 1024 explore / 16384 markdown, "name must not be empty"). Verified: full go gate green + live 29/29 route probe (KC password grant). **P2 (9e318d3):** `dashboards/+page.svelte` rewritten as the **list** page (one row per dashboard: name, widget count, Open link, inline rename, confirm-delete + a create row); `dashboards/[id]/+page.svelte` new **editor**: loads the row via the list + widgets + `loadCatalogs()` name maps; the 12-col grid; widget card = header (size select, move left/right, rename, Open-in-Explore link for explore widgets via `serializeStateForLink`, confirm-delete) + body (`<ExploreView state={parseState(w.content)} names={names}/>` or `marked.parse` → `DOMPurify.sanitize` → `@html`, `$derived`); add-widget row (name + kind buttons — "Explore view" seeds content '', "Text (markdown)" opens a textarea — Enter/Escape save/cancel like the settings cards); dynamic span classes via a `SPAN_CLASSES` const array of all 12 literals (Tailwind JIT only emits literal class strings present in source). Explore page: `window.prompt` save-as-card → inline **save-to-dashboard panel** (dashboard `<select>` from `listDashboards`; zero dashboards → create-name field that creates first, then saves; `createWidget(…, {kind: 'explore', content: serializeState(explore), span: 4})`); `lib/api/dashboards.ts` new (9 client fns + `setWidgetOrder`), `dashboardCards.ts` deleted; i18n rework: 31 `dashboards.*` keys + reworked `explore.*` save wording. **Decision — SvelteKit 3's typed `resolve()` breaks on the new parameterized route:** kit 3.1.1 types it `resolve(...args: ResolveArgs<T>)` — for a `/`-prefixed T whose `RouteParams` are non-empty the 2nd arg is a REQUIRED params object, so once `/dashboards/[id]` entered `RouteId`, two pre-existing call sites broke: the `auth/callback` target cast (the full `RouteId | \`RouteId?${string}\`` union now includes a member that demands params) and the list page's template-literal href `resolve(\`/dashboards/${id}\`)` (a template literal strips to no `RouteId` → `ResolveArgs = [never]`). Fix (runtime-safe: runtime `resolve()` only treats BRACKETED ids as routes; any other absolute path is a plain base-prefixed pathname): the callback now casts to `Exclude<RouteId, '/dashboards/[id]'> | \`…?${string}\`` (parameterized id excluded) and the list page uses the typed param overload `resolve('/dashboards/[id]', { id: d.id })`. **Non-goals (v1):** no drag-and-drop; no in-place editing of an explore widget's saved query (Open-in-Explore loads the state; a changed view is saved as a NEW widget via the panel); no widget kinds beyond explore+markdown (the kind enum + content column make adding one a follow-up); no per-widget data caching/sharing (each widget runs its own paged fetch). **Verify (P3, all green):** svelte-check 0 errors / 0 warnings + vite build (pre-existing echarts >500kB chunk warning only); page walk on :18082 — /dashboards, /dashboards/<uuid>, /explore all 200 (adapter-static SPA fallback) and the exact calls the pages make (`GET /api/v1/dashboards`, `GET /api/v1/dashboards/<uuid>/widgets`) 200 with the right rows through the vite /api proxy (Default dashboard, 1 widget; Test Card explore, span 4, `from=2026-01-01&to=2026-12-31`).

- 2026-11-10: dashboard editor polish (round 7) — two fixes to the round-6 editor (user feedback: (1) on a 16:9 1080p display the widget controls "render just off to the right, bleeding out of the bounds of the card" for a 4-column widget; (2) "resizing by column width is a bit jenky, being able to drag the edges like desktop window resizing (drag sides to extend/shrink in 1 dimension, drag in corners to adjust in 2 at the same time) would be ideal"). **Header overflow:** the per-widget control row (width select + move/open/edit/delete buttons) no longer lives on the card face — the header is now name (truncated, flex-1) + kind badge + a ⋯ overflow menu (local `menuId` $state; `fixed inset-0` click-catcher backdrop + a window Escape $effect; panel `absolute right-0 top-full`), holding Open-in-Explore, width/height selects, Move left/right, Edit, Delete. **Drag resize:** the column-span preset survives only as the a11y fallback inside that menu; the primary resize is now pointer-driven like desktop windows — right-edge handle = width, bottom-edge = height, corner = both (pointer capture on the handle element; units computed from the live grid rect: `colUnit = (gridWidth + 16) / 12`, fixed row pitch 120px + 16px gap = 136). Live preview comes from local `dragging` state — the stored widget is NEVER mutated mid-drag, so a failed commit just drops the preview (that IS the revert) and a single PATCH `{span, row_span}` lands on pointerup. **Height** needed a backend `row_span` (0014: int 1..8 DEFAULT 4, markdown backfilled to 2; per-kind create defaults explore 4 / markdown 2; PATCH absent-or-null = untouched; 400 `row_span must be between 1 and 8` appended LAST to the "; "-joined validation list) and a fixed row pitch (`md:auto-rows-[120px]` + per-widget `row-span-N`, literal class maps for both dimensions; widget body scrolls internally via `min-h-0 flex-1 overflow-y-auto`). **Scope note:** this supersedes the round-6 non-goal "span presets cover resize" in `docs/DASHBOARDS-REWORK.md` (drag-based resize is now user-demanded); drag-and-drop REPOSITIONING remains out of scope (the Move buttons stay). **Verification:** full go gate green (migrate/repo/server/fisync); live probe of the wire contract (create defaults, boundaries 1/8, 400 + combined-error ordering, span-only PATCH leaves row_span untouched); svelte-check 0 errors 0 warnings; `npm run build` green; page walk confirmed the new module compiles with the drag/menu markers and the drag-commit PATCH shape round-trips through the /api proxy.
- 2026-11-10: dashboard editor fixes (round 8) — three fixes (user feedback: (1) "markdown isn't rendering properly, i added a test widget and it is not rendering a title correctly, nor my bullets"; (2) "we should not enforce having to name a widget. esp for markdown, users may just want the title to be 'in markdown' so it renders in a larger font"; (3) "one cannot drag widgets/cards around the dashboard to reorder them. that is essential functionality"). **Markdown typography:** Tailwind v4's preflight strips the unstyled marked output (h1/h2 at body size, bullets at default list spacing); fix is the `@tailwindcss/typography` devDependency + `@plugin "@tailwindcss/typography";` in `app.css`, with the rendered body wrapped in `<article class="prose prose-sm dark:prose-invert">`. **Optional widget names:** dashboard names stay required; widget names are now cap-only (255 runes, no non-empty rule) — repo `validateWidgetName` and server `checkWidgetName` replace `validateName`/`checkName` in widget Create/Update/patch only; stored names are trimmed and `""` round-trips (names are DEK-encrypted bytea, no DB cap); the editor shows a muted-italic "Untitled" for nameless cards, the name placeholder reads "(optional)", and `saveEdit` trims before comparing. **Drag reorder:** the card's header row is a pointer-drag surface (cursor-grab + touch-none; `role="group"` + aria-label + title for the svelte a11y rule; `startMove` ignores presses on interactive children and while editing / a menu is open / another drag is live); window-level pointermove/up/cancel instead of element capture because the node re-parents mid-drag; drop index = top-left-triangle test on the first containing card (before/after); a `displayWidgets` derived splices the dragged card into the live drop slot (no placeholder); one `PUT /widgets/order` with the raw id array on release (no-op iff the drop equals the origin), local `sort_order` rewritten to k+1 on success, `opError` with no mutation on failure; the ⋯ menu Move left/right stay as the a11y fallback. **Scope note:** supersedes round 7's "drag-and-drop REPOSITIONING remains out of scope (the Move buttons stay)" — reordering is now user-demanded; round-7 drag-resize is unchanged. **Verification:** full go gate green (build + vet + migrate/repo/server/fisync tests); live walk through the /api proxy — editor page 200, round-8 module markers compiled into the vite module (startMove/displayWidgets/dragMove/computeDropIndex/persistReorder/data-widget-id/cursor-grab/prose/widget_untitled), prose CSS served with the typography rules, nameless POST → 201 with `""` round-trip, whitespace PATCH trims to `""`, over-255 name → 400 `invalid_request`, PUT /order reverse→verify→restore (204s), markdown fixture intact, probe cleaned up; svelte-check 0 errors 0 warnings; `npm run build` green.
- 2026-11-10: token-expiry UX + configurable access-token lifespan (round 9) — user feedback: (1) "when the user's auth token expires, they just get a red json on the page they're looking at. i understand it, but an average user will not. instead we should pop up a box saying they need to re-log, and have a button that'll direct them to the sign-in page"; (2) "i will note that tokens should last 'a while', ideally perhaps a week? let this be an env var we can tweak easily in the app's config"; (3) "the UX for moving cards isn't ideal, as the UI doesn't show anything until you actually get a card to snap to a new position. the card should move with the cursor as you're dragging it around". **Session-expired dialog:** backend token-failure 401s are the auth0 go-jwt-middleware v3 default body (flat `{"error":"invalid_token","error_description":"...","error_code":"token_malformed"|"token_expired"}` + RFC 6750 WWW-Authenticate), NOT the app's nested 3.1 envelope (that shape is writeAPIError-only and 401s never reach those handlers) — so the dialog keys on the 401 STATUS, not the body. `lib/api/client.ts` gains a pluggable `setUnauthorizedHandler` fired on every 401 before the ApiError is thrown; `lib/auth/client.ts` installs it at module load and only flips `sessionExpired` when `currentUser` exists (a hard reload with no local session still just lands on the sign-in page, no double popup); `+layout.svelte` renders the dialog (`role="alertdialog"`, z-50 — above the z-40 drag ghost) with "Not now" and "Sign in", the latter passing the current `page.url.pathname + search` as the post-login redirect so the user lands back on the page they were on. **Access-token lifespan:** `FTRK_ACCESS_TOKEN_LIFESPAN` (seconds, default 604800 = 1 week) added to the compose keycloak env + `.env.example`; `dev-init.sh` validates it is a positive integer BEFORE boot so a `.env` typo fails the container loudly instead of 400-ing at sync time. Root cause of the original "tokens keep expiring" bug: `finance-realm.json` sets NO lifespan fields, so the realm ran on Keycloak's global defaults (300s token / 36000s SSO max — effective token life was 5 minutes, not the assumed 60s). Keycloak 26.8.1's realm admin endpoint has NO PATCH (verified: 405), so the per-boot sync is a GET-modify-PUT round-trip over the raw admin API (the KC image ships no curl/wget/awk — pure-bash /dev/tcp + sed): fetch the full realm JSON (compact single-line, Content-Length), sed-rewrite three quote+colon-anchored fields — `accessTokenLifespan` AND `ssoSessionMaxLifespan` AND `ssoSessionMaxLifespanRememberMe` in lockstep, because `expires_in = min(accessTokenLifespan, ssoSessionMaxLifespan)` so the session cap would otherwise shrink the token again — guard that all three rewrites landed (a future KC that pretty-prints or renames a field would otherwise 204 an unchanged copy back and silently keep the short default), then PUT the full realm expecting 204. `ssoSessionIdleTimeout` stays 1800s: an IDLE browser still loses its SSO session after 30 min even with a 7-day token — the dialog is the safety net for that path (noted, not changed; the ask was token life, not idle timeout). **Drag ghost (`dashboards/[id]`):** the dragged card no longer sits in its origin slot until release — its root stays in the grid flow at the live drop slot (no placeholder swap, so the card's content components don't remount mid-drag) and renders as a dashed sky "hole" (dashed border, head/body/resize handles hidden) while moving; a fixed-position ghost clone (pointer-events-none, z-40, offset by the grab point so the card doesn't jump to the cursor, sized to the card's measured rect) follows the cursor on every pointermove and mirrors the card head + first markdown line; on release the hole/ghost collapse and the single /order PATCH fires as before.

- 2026-11-10: session-expiry follow-ups (round 10) — post-login return_to + the zombie "session expired" dialog (user feedback: "my test session had the popup, so i logged in, and when i came back, the popup came right back, refreshing didn't clear it, i had to click sign in and have it 'do nothing' to dismiss it"; "it also brought me to the homepage instead of 'the page i was on'"). **Bug B (homepage redirect):** the round-9 `return_to` authorize param was dead weight — Keycloak does NOT echo custom authorize query params on the callback redirect (a live probe that replays the browser's exact code+PKCE flow with curl, /tmp/opencode/r10_code_flow.sh, confirmed the callback URL carries only code/state/session_state/iss, and the login form's client_data base64 is just {ru, rt, st}), so `page.url.search.get('return_to')` was always null and every successful login fell back to '/'. Fix: the target is stashed in localStorage (`ftrk-return-to`) by login() BEFORE the redirect (a no-target login clears it); /auth/callback reads it with readReturnTo() (the same-origin relative-path validation is preserved — reject absolute URLs and protocol-relative '//evil.com') and clearReturnTo()s it right before the successful goto; a FAILED exchange keeps the stash so a retry still lands the user where they were; the false "Keycloak echoes it back" comments were corrected in both files. **Bug A (zombie dialog):** on the callback document the layout's initSession() restores the PRE-LOGIN persisted user (expired token — that is why we re-authenticated) in a microtask, and the sub-keyed settings effect then fires GET /v1/settings with the stale token → 401 → the round-9 handler sees a session present locally and raises sessionExpired over "Signing in…", and nothing ever clears it. "Refreshing didn't clear it" proved the exchange had not persisted a valid user: prime suspect is a DOUBLE-CLICKED Sign in — signinRedirect() is async (OIDC discovery before it writes the state and navigates), so a second click inside that window overwrites the stateStore entry with a NEW state while the browser commits to the FIRST navigation → state mismatch → signinRedirectCallback() throws → the stale user stays in userStore → every reload re-restores it and re-raises the dialog; the user's retry "did nothing" because the Keycloak SSO session was already alive (instant round trip) and that finally succeeded. **Fixes (frontend only, lib/auth/client.ts + the callback page):** (1) signInCallback() sets a signInInFlight flag for the duration of the exchange, and the 401 handler never raises the dialog while it is set OR within a 5-second post-success grace window (a late 401 from the stale-token settings GET can still land after the exchange resolves); (2) on success the dialog flag is explicitly cleared; (3) on failure the stale user is removed (await c.removeUser() + store null) so the error card renders over a clean signed-out state whose pages show their normal fail-open prompts instead of a zombie popup; (4) login() is guarded by a redirectInFlight flag (5-second backstop timer, reset on rejection) so a double-click can no longer race two states into the stateStore. The probe also re-confirmed the code-flow token is fully valid (expires_in 604800, aud=finance-api — the realm's audience mapper adds it, so the `audience` authorize param / PUBLIC_AUTH0_AUDIENCE remain harmless no-ops and were left untouched) and the backend accepts it (200 on /api/v1/dashboards), ruling out the token path as the dialog's source. svelte-check 0/0, vite build green; no backend or Keycloak changes.
- 2026-11-10: dashboard drag bounds (round 11) — make the 12-col grid bounds visible while a widget is being dragged so the width limit is visible (user feedback: "when one drags tiles around, they cannot see the bounds of the dashboard, so it can look visually like 'there is space to put one beside another', but there's a restriction on the width in reality, so they can never get it to snap"). The problem: during a header drag-move the ghost follows the pointer anywhere on the screen and the card re-renders as a dashed hole at the live drop slot, but the grid has no visible edges — the empty space past a wide card looks like room to drop "beside" it, yet a card that does not fit in the row's remaining columns wraps to the next row, so it never snaps where the user aimed. Fix (frontend-only, dashboards/[id]/+page.svelte, display-only — no persistence, no backend/Keycloak change): while a drag-move is live (1) the grid div gains `ring-2 ring-sky-500/40` (a box-shadow ring — zero layout impact) marking the dashboard's outer edge, and (2) a new overlay child draws the 11 column dividers — an absolutely-positioned `inset-0` div with NO grid area (its containing block is the grid's padding box, so it affects no track sizing), `pointer-events-none` so it never steals a card/handle pointer target, `aria-hidden`, md+ only (below md the grid is one stacked column with no dividers). The divider x-offsets are computed from the grid's content width — a new `gridW` field on the dragMove state, seeded at pointerdown and re-read on EVERY pointermove so a mid-drag window resize moves the dividers with the grid — placing the i-th divider in the MIDDLE of the i-th gap: i full tracks plus (i−1/2) gaps with track = (gridW − 11·16)/12, reusing the GRID_COLS/GRID_GAP constants that are kept in sync with the grid's Tailwind classes. The sky palette matches the existing drag affordances (dashed hole sky-500/60, ring /40, dividers /25 — subtle enough not to obscure card content). No new i18n strings (purely visual). Gate: svelte-check 0/0, vite build green.
- 2026-11-10: dashboard drag bounds — column slot frames (round 12) — replace the round-11 mid-gap divider lines with 12 full-height column slot frames at a card's EXACT footprint width, so a full-span card visibly flush-fills its column instead of looking "a bit smaller than a full column width" (user feedback: "okay, but now i can see that my tile doesn't align perfectly, it's a bit smaller than a full colummn width. we should have resizes snap to full columns if we're gonna have a column system, that will help users make better dashboards, and also just 'look better' when they're dragging stuff around and see 'oh hey i'm only using 80% of this column, that looks weird'"). **Diagnosis:** the resize snap was ALREADY whole-column — startDrag computes `span = clampSpan(startSpan + Math.round(Δx / colUnit))` with `colUnit = (rect.width + GRID_GAP) / GRID_COLS` = track + gap = the grid's exact pitch, so spans are always integers 1..12; nothing snapped wrong. The perceived misalignment was the overlay itself: the round-11 dividers sit in the MIDDLE of each 16px gap, so the perceived column (divider-to-divider = track + gap) is 16px WIDER than the card's footprint (track) — at a 1200px-wide grid a 12-span card fills only ~84% of its perceived column, exactly the "only using 80%" the user saw. **Fix (frontend-only, dashboards/[id]/+page.svelte, display-only — no behavior/persistence/i18n/backend change):** the 11 mid-gap lines are replaced by 12 column SLOT frames, one per column, each at the card's exact footprint width: slot i (0-based) spans `[i·colUnit, i·colUnit + track)` with `colUnit = (gridW + GRID_GAP)/GRID_COLS` (track + gap — the exact pitch the resize snap uses) and `track = (gridW − (GRID_COLS−1)·GRID_GAP)/GRID_COLS`, so the last slot's right edge lands on gridW exactly, a card flush-fills its slot, and the 16px gutters sit BETWEEN slots, never inside them. Frames: `absolute inset-y-0 rounded-lg border border-sky-500/40 bg-sky-500/5` (rounded-lg = the card's 8px radius; the frame's 1px border lands exactly on the card's 1px border), reusing the round-11 overlay mechanics unchanged — an absolutely-positioned child with NO grid area (containing block = the grid's padding box, so no track sizing impact), pointer-events-none, aria-hidden, md+ only, gridW re-read on every pointermove. The grid ring (dashboard outer edge) is kept. The `gridLines` derived is renamed `gridSlots` returning `{left, width}[]`. Gate: svelte-check 0/0, vite build green.

- 2026-11-10: dashboard tiles — headless untitled cards (round 13) — for a nameless tile, hide the title header bar and show only the contents, so a markdown tile's own `# Title` renders at the top without a small "Untitled" bar above it (user feedback: "next little nit, if a tile is untitled, then don't show the title header for the tile, just show the contents (that way if one has a markdown tile that has a title in it, that shows right at the top w/o a bar that just says 'untitled' in small text above it)"). **Why hide, not delete:** the card head is the ONLY home of the ⋯ menu (rename / delete / open-in-Explore / resize selects / move) AND the round-8 drag-move surface (`startMove` on the row's `onpointerdown`), so it cannot be removed from the DOM — it can only be made invisible. **Mechanics (frontend-only, dashboards/[id]/+page.svelte, display-only — no persistence/backend/i18n change):** the card root gains a `group` class, and a NAMELESS card's head is out of the flow at rest via `sr-only` — NOT `display:none`/`hidden`, because that would make the ⋯ button un-focusable and lock keyboard users out of renaming / deleting a nameless card. The head slides back into the flow when the card is hovered or has focus (`group-hover:not-sr-only group-focus-within:not-sr-only`), and unconditionally while its menu is open or it is being edited. The head's class is now the ternary `moving ? 'hidden' : w.name === '' ? (menuId === w.id || editingId === w.id ? '' : 'sr-only group-hover:not-sr-only group-focus-within:not-sr-only') : ''` — titled cards keep the row exactly as before. **Cascade trap (verified in the built CSS):** Tailwind v4 emits the variant rules `.group-hover\:not-sr-only:is(:where(.group):hover *)` / `.group-focus-within\:not-sr-only:is(:where(.group):focus-within *)` AFTER the base `.sr-only` with higher specificity, so the reveal wins on every sr-only property — but the reveal also sets `padding:0`, which likewise beats the head row's own `px-3 py-1.5` ((0,2,0) > (0,1,0)), so the revealed head would sit flush against the card edges. Fix: the padding moved OFF the sr-only-toggling row ONTO its children — the left child (name / badge / edit input) carries `px-3 py-1.5`, and the right-side branches (edit-mode Save / Cancel buttons and the `relative` ⋯ wrapper that anchors the menu panel's `absolute right-0 top-full`) are wrapped in a new `flex shrink-0 items-center gap-2 py-1.5 pr-3` container; the `pr-3` keeps the panel 12px inside the card's right edge, exactly as before, and a titled card's row height is unchanged (32px + 1px border either way). **Kept:** the `dashboards.widget_untitled` key (the revealed head's muted-italic "Untitled" placeholder, the drag ghost — which deliberately keeps its head — and the delete-confirm label in `removeWidget`). Gate: svelte-check 0/0, vite build green.
- 2026-11-10: budgets — history renamed + period-aware window naming (round 14) — user feedback after building a budget: "in the adherence history (should just be called history btw), we show the window and a date range. we should be more intelligent, call the column month/quarter/year depending on the budget's period, and then just have each entry be the friendlier name for that range based on the period type (e.g. `March 2025`, `Q4 2023`, or `2024`)". **Changes (frontend-only, display-only — no backend/Go change):** `en.json` — `budgets.history` "Adherence history" → "History"; the now-unused `budgets.window_label` ("{from} – {to}") and `budgets.col_window` ("Window") deleted (both were used only by the history table; `budgets.current_window` "for {from} – {to}" is kept for the current-period label). `budgets/+page.svelte` — the window column's header reuses the existing `periodLabel(b.period)` (already returns "Month" / "Quarter" / "Year" from `budgets.period_month/quarter/year`), so no new key, and a new `windowName(from, period)` helper next to `periodLabel` derives each row's label from the window's start date: year → `String(y)` ("2024"); quarter → `Q${Math.floor((m-1)/3)+1} ${y}` ("Q4 2023"); month → `Intl.DateTimeFormat($locale ?? 'en', { month: 'long', year: 'numeric' })` ("March 2025") — the import is now `import { locale, _ } from 'svelte-i18n';` and the `?? 'en'` fallback exists because svelte-i18n 4 types the locale store as possibly null before init (the app is English-only per the i18n registration comment, so this is future-proofing). **Why the exact range was dropped (no tooltip kept):** the backend's trend windows are whole calendar periods (`periodBounds` in `backend/internal/server/summary.go` — month = calendar month, quarter = the 3-month block, year = calendar year, all UTC, `from`/`to` via `dateOnly`), so a window's start date names the window fully and the old "from – to" text was redundant. Gate: svelte-check 0/0, vite build green.
- 2026-11-10: budgets — friendly "For …" label + variance-from-target in braces (round 15) — user feedback: "the 'FOR' text under the budget that describes the active month: has FOR in all caps, which is weird. PascalCase is fine; doesn't use the friendlier naming, still is a raw date range. i also see that for a month where the amount is over (the only data point i have), it just shows as red. this DOES indicate that someone is over, but not by how much. it would be good to see, perhaps in braces (e.g. `$344.03 (55%)`) how much above/below the budgeted amount that is". **Interpretation confirmed against live data:** the user's only over-budget data point is the October 2026 history row (Groceries $513.04 vs the $200 target ≈ 157% over — hence the red cell), and the example "(55%)" must be the VARIANCE from the budgeted amount (a 55%-of-target ratio could not be an over month), so the figure is `round(|spend − target| / target · 100)` with the sign carried by the existing red/green treatment, never by the number. **Changes (frontend-only, display-only — no backend/Go change):** `en.json` — `budgets.current_window` "for {from} – {to}" → "For {window}". `budgets/+page.svelte` — (1) the label's `uppercase` class is dropped and its value is the current window's friendly name (e.g. "For November 2026") via a new `currentWindowName(b)` display helper next to `windowName` (returns `windowName(s.from, b.period)`, or `''` while the summary is absent — the card only renders once its summary has loaded, so this is always named in practice); (2) a new `deltaPct(spend, target)` GEOMETRY-ONLY helper alongside the other cents helpers (`Math.round(|toCents(spend) − targetCents(target)| / t · 100`, null when there is no spend or the target is not positive — the page-header rendered-numbers contract comment now lists it among the geometry-only parsers); (3) the figure renders as a muted `({d}%)` right after the spend string in BOTH places the spend appears — the current-period row (a `text-zinc-400` span inside the row's tabular-nums span, ahead of the existing " / target" suffix; the row keeps its bar + Over/Under label as the direction signal) and the history-table cell (a plain `<span>` so it inherits the cell's red-when-over treatment — October now reads "513.04 (157%)" in red). No new i18n keys (the figure is a bare whole number; svelte-i18n's `{d}` interpolation of a number needs no formatting key). **Svelte/TS gotchas hit:** the first inline attempt `summaries[b.period] ? windowName(summaries[b.period].from, …) : ''` fails svelte-check — TS drops the truthiness narrowing of an indexed access inside a function argument ("Object is possibly 'undefined'") — and the `{@const cur = …}` fix is rejected in turn (`{@const}` must be an immediate child of a block, not of a `<p>`), hence the plain script helper. Gate: svelte-check 0/0, vite build green.
- 2026-11-10: saved Explore views (round 16) — users save named queries+views in Explore and pick from them (or make a new one) when adding a dashboard widget (user feedback: "next up: users should be able to save queries+views in 'explore', and then when they go to a dashboard, when they want to add one, they can pick from that list of saved things OR click a button to make a new one, rather than right now, where it takes 'what explore is currently doing', which feels kinda jenk"). **Migration 0015**: `saved_views(id uuid PK DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(), user_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE, name_encrypted bytea NOT NULL, state_encrypted bytea NOT NULL, created_at/updated_at timestamptz)` + `saved_views_user_idx (user_id)`; down = DROP TABLE. Like the dashboards, names have NO unique constraint (duplicate labels allowed); `name` and `state` are each ONE DEK pack — the state can carry the free-text `q` term, like the old `dashboard_cards.explore_state` did. **API** (`internal/server/savedviews.go`, bootstrap-DEK pass-through like the dashboards): `GET /v1/saved-views` → `[{id,name,state}]` created_at asc; `POST {name,state}` → 201 (name trimmed; blank → 400 "name must not be empty"); `PATCH /v1/saved-views/{id} {name?,state?}` → 200 (empty patch → 400 "empty patch: provide at least one field"; non-UUID id → 404 "saved view not found" AFTER requireUser and BEFORE decode; state > 1024 runes → 400 "state must be at most 1024 characters"); `DELETE` → 204. No single-GET (house convention), and the server does NOT re-validate the state format — the frontend codec is the canonical-form source of truth, the server only bounds the size. **Repo** (`repo.SavedViewStore`): List/Create/Update/Delete (no Get); a no-op update (single unchanged field) returns the row WITHOUT an UPDATE, so `updated_at` stays stable. **Copy-not-reference (the core decision):** a widget's content is a COPY of the picked saved view's state, not a reference — deleting a saved view never breaks a widget. **Frontend:** new `api/savedviews.ts` client. Explore view bar: a "Saved views" button next to "Save to dashboard" toggles a panel under the save panel — a "Save current view" row (name input + Save posts `serializeState(explore)`; `''` = the default view; Cancel) and one row per view with **Open** (`explore = parseState(v.state)` — the existing sync effect rewrites the URL and remembered settings), **Update** (PATCH `{state}` — refreshes the stored state to what Explore currently shows), inline **Rename** (autofocus action; blank/unchanged cancels), and confirm-gated **Delete**; loads are seq-guarded, a load failure degrades to the empty list (saving still works), notes self-clear after 2.5s. Dashboard add-widget flow: `startAdd('explore')` loads the list and the picker renders for the explore kind only — "None" (`''` → the old empty-state path), "New" (the typed widget name becomes the new saved view's name, created with `state: ''`; a blank name surfaces the server's 400 verbatim), or one option per saved view (its state is copied into the new widget; `span: 4`). i18n: `explore.saved_views` + 14 `view_*` keys, `dashboards.widget_add_saved_view/none/new`. Gate: go test green (repo 48.3s, server 24.7s, migrate 10.9s incl. the v15 up/re-up/down lifecycle), live CRUD against the rebuilt stack (list 200, POST 201, PATCH state 200, PATCH name 200, DELETE 204, non-UUID 404, blank-name 400), svelte-check 0/0, vite build green.

- 2026-11-10: "Add a tile" — titled single-row tile picker + the Explore save-to handoff (round 17) — the dashboard's bottom add area becomes a titled one-row picker, and "Create new view" hands off to the Explore page, where naming + saving a view lands it back on the dashboard as both a saved view and a tile (user feedback: "the 'add widget' area in the bottom of the dashboard is disjointed rn, it should: - have a name (add tile or smth) - only have 1 row, not two - that row should just have the dropdown, add, and cancel options - the dropdown should have a all saved views, add current view, and create new view - the latter option would bring them to the view page, where they would make whatever query they wanted, and hit save to dashboard, and that would send them back to the dashboard, with that now saved as a view, and added to their dashboard"). **Confirmed interpretations:** (1) the round-13 markdown note survives as a dropdown option — "Markdown note" is the ONLY choice that reveals fields (an optional name + the textarea) below the row; (2) "Add current view" is an UNTITLED quick-add — it creates a nameless tile (rendered "Untitled") from the Explore page's remembered last view, and no saved view is created; a user who wants a named view uses "Create new view". **Dashboard (frontend-only, dashboards/[id]/+page.svelte):** the at-rest box is now the title ("Add a tile") + ONE button; opened it is the title + ONE row — [picker] [Add] [Cancel] — replacing the two kind buttons and the two-row name/picker card. The picker offers one option per saved view (the tile takes the view's name and a COPY of its state — the round-16 copy-not-reference rule stands), then "Add current view" (content = the remembered state read straight from localStorage; '' = the default view; no saved view created), "Create new view" (NOT an add — `goto('/explore?save_to=<dashboardId>')` and return), and "Markdown note" (the typed, optional name + the typed note). Add is disabled until a choice is made (the placeholder option is disabled); Cancel closes the box; the round-16 inline "new saved view" path (the 'new' sentinel + its createSavedView call) is gone — view creation now lives only in the Explore handoff. **Explore handoff (explore/+page.svelte):** arriving as /explore?save_to=<id> with a session opens the save panel pre-aimed at that dashboard — the target is LOCKED: the dashboard select and the new-dashboard path don't render, replaced by a muted "Saving to {name}" line (shown only when the name loaded — saving needs only the id, so a list failure doesn't block). Saving in handoff mode runs createSavedView({name, state}) AND createWidget(saveToId, {name, kind: 'explore', content: state, span: 4}) — the view's name names the tile too — then `goto('/dashboards/<id>')` (push, not replace — the browser back still works); Cancel just closes the panel. The handoff lives only in a `saveToId` const read at init: the existing URL-sync effect strips ALL query params from the address bar on its first run, so the param is never re-parsed or shareable; a one-shot `handoffArmed` flag behind the $user guard arms it exactly once (on sign-in). **Shared key:** `EXPLORE_STATE_KEY = 'ftrk-explore-state'` moves from a page-local const in explore/+page.svelte to an export in lib/explore/state.ts — the single named contract between the page that writes it (every URL-sync) and the dashboard's "Add current view", which reads it. **i18n (en.json):** added `dashboards.widget_add_title` ("Add a tile"), `dashboards.widget_add_choice` ("Choose a tile…"), `dashboards.widget_add_current` ("Add current view"), `dashboards.widget_add_new_view` ("Create new view"), `explore.save_handoff_target` ("Saving to {name}"); `dashboards.widget_add_markdown` "Text (markdown)" → "Markdown note" (it is now a dropdown option); removed `dashboards.widget_add` ("Add a widget:"), `widget_add_explore`, `widget_add_saved_view`, `widget_add_saved_none`, `widget_add_saved_new` (all confined to the replaced block). **Backend: ZERO work** — the round-16 saved-views API and the widget CRUD carry the whole flow. Gate: svelte-check 0/0, vite build green.

- 2026-11-10: any-number-of-categories filter in Explore (round 18) — the category filter now mirrors the multi-account one (user feedback: "looks like in explore, you can select 'any number of' accounts (unconfirmed since we only have 1 in our test data, but the UI implies this), but one must select 'all or just one category', we should enable 'any number of' categories as well"). **Contract:** `ExploreState.category` goes `string` → `string[]` with exactly the `account` shape — stored AS WRITTEN (case preserved), in URL order, no dedup; `'none'` = uncategorized only, empty array = any category. The URL `category` param is repeatable (every valid occurrence kept, in order), and `apiQuery` sends one `category_id` per value (`'none'` → `''`); `ListParams.category_id` becomes `string[]` appended once per value, exactly like `account_id`. Every `ExploreState` construction goes through `parseState` (URL, remembered state, saved-view state, widget state — the round-17 copy), so legacy single-`category` URLs and saved/remembered states parse as one-element lists with NO migration or data work. **Backend:** `TransactionFilter.CategoryID *string` → `CategoryIDs []string`; List builds one OR group from all values — `category_id IS NULL` per `''` value and/or `category_id IN (...)` — appended bare when single, parenthesized `(... OR ...)` when multiple; `txFilterFromQuery` loops ALL `category_id` values (each `''` or UUID-shaped, else 400 `category_id must be a valid UUID` — replaces "first value applies"). **UI:** the category select is `multiple` (`h-16 w-56`, `<div>` wrapper mirroring the accounts block); the "All categories" option is dropped (no selection = any); new `onCategoriesChange` mirrors `onAccountsChange`; `resetFilters` uses `category: []`. i18n: `explore.category_any` removed; `explore.category_label` "Category" → "Categories" (the manual-entry block is untouched). **Tests:** the server List subtest becomes "category_id: repeatable, OR-combined; empty=uncategorized" with two new cases (c1+c2 → their union; empty+c1 → uncategorized OR c1); the repo filter table gains `categorized OR uncategorized` (all rows), `category no match` (empty), and `category no match OR uncategorized`; the memTxStore mirror ORs over `CategoryIDs`. Gate: go test ./... green, svelte-check 0/0, vite build green.

- 2026-11-10: resizable, default-all-selected Explore filter boxes + richer dev data (round 19) — the account and category filter boxes are now resizable checkbox listboxes that default to all-selected (user feedback: "the categories and accounts boxes in the filter section of the explore tab should be resizable so people can view larger sets more easily / they should default to all selected for ease-of-use / also can we just generate more test data (multiple accounts, more transactions, etc)? would make more testing easier. when you're done, definitely do make sure our containers are up-to-date, can't test our fixes without the code updated with our fixes!"). **UI (frontend-only):** a native `<select multiple>` cannot be CSS-resized, so both become div-based checkbox listboxes: `resize-y overflow-y-auto` (resize requires non-visible overflow), default `h-24 min-h-16 w-56`, `bg-white dark:bg-zinc-900`, each row a `<label>` + checkbox + truncated name; the category box leads with the Uncategorized (`'none'`) row, then one row per category. **Default-all-selected is display-only** — the state contract is unchanged (empty `string[]` = any; no API/codec/URL/saved-view work): an empty selection renders every option checked; unchecking one materializes the complement (all ids minus the toggled one); re-selecting the full set canonicalizes back to `[]` so URLs stay clean and later-created accounts/categories are included automatically. `onAccountsChange`/`onCategoriesChange` are replaced by `toggleAccount`/`toggleCategory` over a shared `toggleSelection(current, all, id, checked)`; `resetFilters`/init already produce `[]`.
