A `selectionTick` $state counter bumps on every toggle so the rows re-mount and the DOM can never drift from state (e.g. unchecking the last explicit id canonicalizes to empty and renders all-checked). **Dev data:** new `scripts/seed-dev-data.py` — reusable, stdlib-only, same `FTRK_*` env conventions as `verify-authn.sh` (authenticates itself via the Keycloak password grant; no manual token needed); idempotently ensures 5 accounts (Everyday Checking, High-Yield Savings, Store Card, Cash Wallet, TFSA — covering the bank/credit_card/cash/investment type vocabulary), 6 categories (Salary, Interest, Freelance, Subscriptions, Health, Home), and ~280 deterministic transactions over 2026-03-01→2026-11-10 (biweekly payroll, quarterly interest, 8 freelance deposits, the rest weighted spending across categories, ~8% uncategorized, ~15% with notes, 4 review-pending); rows use a fixed RNG seed, so a re-run hits the 0002 dedup keys on its own rows and the 409s count as skips — verified first run 280 created / 0 skipped, re-run 0 created / 280 skipped (total 302 rows). Gate: svelte-check 0/0, vite build green (no backend change → backend gate skipped). Containers: full `compose up --build -d` to HEAD — the worker (and one-shot migrate) were still on pre-R18 images and were recreated; the backend/frontend images were already at HEAD. Live smoke: 6 accounts, 15 categories, 302 rows (paging through `offset=200`), multi-`category_id` OR (Salary+Freelance → exactly their 26 rows, all income), `category_id=` → 5 uncategorized rows, and the vite dev server serves the new listbox markup.

- 2026-11-11: dashboard-owned time range — decoupled from the cards' saved queries (round 20) — the dashboard page, not the saved query, now owns the date range the cards query with: a date range picker in the page's top right re-ranges every card at once, and newly saved tiles no longer store a range (user feedback: "next, i want to decouple the time range in queries that are used to build the cards in a dashboard from the dashboard itself. the user should be able to change the time range in the dashboard view, so the queries saved should omit that field, and the dashboard should control that. a date range picker should appear in the top right of the page to allow users to change it as desired"). **Codec (state.ts, pure):** new `withoutRange(qs)` — `parseState`, zero the `from`/`to` fields, `serializeState` (canonical re-serialization; malformed/legacy input degrades exactly like `parseState`, and a range-only input round-trips to `''`). **ExploreView:** two optional PRIMITIVE props `rangeFrom?`/`rangeTo?` — when provided (even as `''`, that end unbounded) they replace the state's `from`/`to` in the fetch effect's filter snapshot; deliberately primitives, NOT a `{from,to}` object prop — an object prop would churn identity on unrelated host re-renders and re-run the fetch effect. Hosts passing nothing (the Explore page) keep the state's dates — its behavior is unchanged. **Dashboard page (dashboards/[id]/+page.svelte):** new page-level `rangeFrom`/`rangeTo` `$state` (both `''` = all time); the header row (now `items-center`) gains, top right under `{#if $user}`, two `type="date"` inputs (classes matching the Explore page's date inputs) labelled `dashboards.range_from`/`range_to`; every explore card renders `<ExploreView state=… names=… rangeFrom={rangeFrom} rangeTo={rangeTo} />` — a picker change re-fetches ALL cards at once, and the all-time default overrides the dates a legacy saved state carries. **Strip-on-save (both sides of the tile-creation path):** `submitAdd`'s "Add current view" stores `withoutRange(rememberedExploreState())` and a picked saved view stores `withoutRange(sv.state)`; the Explore page's `saveToDashboard` stores `withoutRange(state)` as the widget content in BOTH branches (the `?save_to=` handoff and the plain panel) — but the handoff's NAMED saved view keeps the FULL state (it's a normal Explore artifact; re-opening it in Explore restores the dates), and the plain "Saved views" `saveView()` is unchanged. "Open in Explore" is unchanged — it hands off the widget's stored state VERBATIM (a legacy tile's old dates travel with it into Explore; a new tile opens range-less). **i18n:** `dashboards.range_from` ("From") + `dashboards.range_to` ("To"). **No backend work** — the state string is an opaque payload to the server; no e2e/codec_check files exist in this repo. Gates: svelte-check 0 errors / 0 warnings + vite build; no Go changes.

- 2026-11-11: dashboard range picker gains period presets (round 21) — the top-right range picker (round 20) now offers this/last month, this/last quarter, this/last year, and all time in a `Period` select beside the free-form `From`/`To` date inputs (user feedback: "we should add common options like this+last for month, quarter, and year to the range selectors in addition to free-form selection"). **Dashboard page only (dashboards/[id]/+page.svelte):** a preset block mirroring the Explore page's house pattern (browser `Date` — page-only; state.ts stays Date-free): `presetRange(key)` computes full local-time ranges (this month = 1st→last day; quarters = full quarter via `Math.floor(m/3)*3`; `last_quarter` uses a possibly-negative month offset — Jan → last year's Q4 — which the `Date` constructor normalizes), `PRESET_KEYS` (7 keys), `currentPreset()` (the key whose computed range EXACTLY matches the current `rangeFrom`/`rangeTo`, else `'custom'`), `presetLabel(key)` (i18n), and `applyPreset(key)` ('custom' is display-only). **Design:** presets are pure INPUT SUGAR — picking one just writes its dates into the same two `rangeFrom`/`rangeTo` `$state` fields; the select's value is DERIVED from those fields (no separate preset state, so the select always agrees with the date inputs — hand-editing a date flips it to `Custom`, and typing dates that happen to match a preset flips it back). Nothing downstream changes: the cards' re-fetch effect and the strip-on-save path see only the two date fields. The Explore page's own preset set (this/last month, this quarter, this year, all time — no `last_quarter`/`last_year`) is untouched. **i18n:** `dashboards.preset_label` ("Period") + `preset_this_month`/`preset_last_month`/`preset_this_quarter`/`preset_last_quarter`/`preset_this_year`/`preset_last_year`/`preset_all_time`/`preset_custom` (wording matches the existing `explore.preset_*` values). **No backend work.** Gates: svelte-check 0 errors / 0 warnings + vite build; preset date math verified with 18 boundary cases (month ends, Jan year-crossing, quarter boundaries, leap Feb) run under bare node.

- 2026-11-11: mortgage discoverability + seeded live data + principal-mode BasePayment fix (round 22) — exploration verdict on the mortgage feature: fully implemented per plan 7.1–7.3 (commits f4e52dc/7a08649/4a41365) but unreachable in the dev env — plan 7.3's nav existence-gate rendered the Mortgage tab only while a `mortgage`-typed account existed, the dev DB had none, so the tab never appeared and `GET /v1/mortgages` returned `[]`. The user chose "both": (a) make the nav item always visible, (b) seed a mortgage account + mortgage in the live dev DB. **Nav (frontend, AppShell.svelte):** the Mortgage entry is now unconditional — the page's own empty states (sign-in prompt; "no mortgage on file" → Settings → Accounts link; 9-field add form once a mortgage-typed account exists) are the discovery path. **Deviation from plan 7.3 (user-directed):** the existence-gate is retired; with it went the `hasMortgage` state, `refreshMortgage()`, the `visibleNavItems` derived, and the `listAccounts` import — the 60 s interval now refreshes the unread badge only. **Seed data (live, via API):** account "Home Mortgage" (type mortgage, CAD, id 8eb0d8da-fb83-4848-9c5e-57f36814d884) + mortgage id 3e92806f-b088-488a-8a4f-68788f7f3b48 = {principal 485000.00, annual_rate 4.39, amortization_years 25, start_date 2026-01-15, payment_frequency semi_monthly, extra_payment_amount 200.00, extra_payment_frequency monthly, extra_prepayment_type principal}. **Engine bug the seed data exposed (backend, internal/mortgage/mortgage.go):** the `base_payment` KPI reported 505.43 — the schedule's FINAL partial payment — instead of the initial regular payment 1326.18. Root cause: in principal mode `pay` is re-amortized (smaller, same end date) after every extra, and `runSim` returned `round2(pay)` at payoff — the struct-documented "initial regular payment" was the re-amortized final value. Every existing test used "term" mode, where `pay` never mutates, so nothing pinned this. Fix: capture `basePay := pay` before the loop and return `round2(basePay)`; what-if schedules run through the same `runSim`, so the fix covers them (scenario at 3.64% now reports 1227.80, its own initial payment at the new rate). **Regression test:** `TestRecurringExtraPrincipalBasePayment` (mortgage_test.go) pins the seed loan — BasePayment == the no-extra base == 1326.18, final payment < BasePayment (the pre-fix symptom), PayoffIndex 600 (principal mode preserves the term), and payment 13 < payment 1 (re-amortization visibly shrinks the regular portion after a year of extras). **Verified post-rebuild (live probe):** base_payment 1326.18 (was 505.43), all other KPIs unchanged (payoff 2050-08-22 / index 600 / total_interest 292642.33; what-if 3.64% + 400.00/mo → payoff 2050-08-07, months_saved 1, interest_saved 70444.81); `GET /mortgage` → 200 with the nav entry rendering unconditionally. Gates: go test ./... 17/17 packages ok (incl. the new test); svelte-check 0 errors / 0 warnings + vite build; containers rebuilt to HEAD.

- 2026-11-11: mortgage term/renewal engine + backend (round 23) — the user's mortgage model: a renewable **term** ("most canadian mortgages are 25 years and one renews every 5, though both of those values can vary"), variable vs fixed, rate changes as events, and what-if simulation ("simulate what happens if you renew at a different rate, if you change from variable to fixed or vice versa, if you renew early or at the end of your term"). User decisions: transaction matching = **recompute from actuals** (round 25); all three rounds (23 engine+backend → 24 UI → 25 matching) run back-to-back. **No SQL migration:** `term_years`, `rate_type`, `rate_events` live in the existing DEK-encrypted spec blob (migrations 0001–0015; mortgages = 0009) — the blob already parses to a spec struct, so no new column. **Engine (internal/mortgage/mortgage.go):** `Spec` += `TermYears int` (0 = no term), `RateType string` ("" tolerated as fixed — legacy rows, never fabricated on read), `RateEvents []RateEvent{Date, AnnualRate, RateType, Action}`; Action `"re_amortize"` (a renewal: payment = amortize(balance, new rate, remaining amortization) — the end date is preserved) or `"keep_payment"` (the payment is kept, the payoff floats); events take effect on the first payment dated >= event date; event dates strictly ascending; `NextRenewalDate(spec)` = last re_amortize event date (else start) + TermYears, `false` when TermYears <= 0. New runtime error "the payment cannot cover the interest after the rate change", reachable ONLY via keep_payment. **WhatIf (whatsif.go):** `Scenario` += `PaymentFrequency *string` (monthly/semi_monthly/biweekly) and `Renewal *Renewal{Date, AnnualRate, RateType}`; `composeRenewal` keeps stored events strictly BEFORE the renewal date and appends the simulated renewal as re_amortize (it supersedes stored events dated on/after it); `EffectiveSpec` exported so the schedule's next_renewal is computed from the COMPOSED spec. `months_saved` = base.PayoffIndex − scen.PayoffIndex — payment **PERIODS**, so biweekly-vs-monthly is NEGATIVE (−350 for 300 vs 650). **400 vs 500:** the what-if 400 is pinned ONLY to "one-time prepayment date is after the payoff date"; the cover-interest error → 500 — it can only arise from a stored keep_payment event, and the same row 500s on GET amortization, so a 400 here would be inconsistent. **Wire contract:** `term_years` JSON integer 0–40 (default 0); `rate_type` fixed|variable (default "fixed"); `rate_events` = `[{effective_date, annual_rate (canonical 2-decimal string), rate_type, action}]`, always served `[]` (never null); POST: client-sent `rate_events` silently ignored (events only via PATCH — creation is the moment of the initial rate); PATCH: nil = untouched, non-nil = full-array replacement (`[]` clears). Pinned 400 strings: `term_years must be between 0 and 40`; `rate_type must be fixed or variable`; `rate event effective_date must be a date in YYYY-MM-DD form`; `rate event effective_date must be after start_date`; `rate event annual_rate must be a number`; `rate event annual_rate must be greater than 0 and at most 100`; `rate event rate_type must be fixed or variable`; `rate event action must be re_amortize or keep_payment`; `rate event effective_dates must be strictly ascending`; `renewal.date is required`; `renewal.date must be a date in YYYY-MM-DD form`; `renewal.date must be after the start date`; `renewal.annual_rate is required`; `renewal.annual_rate must be a number`; `renewal.annual_rate must be greater than 0 and at most 100`; `renewal.rate_type must be fixed or variable`; `payment_frequency must be monthly, semi_monthly, or biweekly` (wording invented this round — the engine's three frequencies). **Server ordering (test-pinned):** the PATCH update handler Gets the row BEFORE field validation (the events-after-stored-start check) — an unknown id 404s even with a malformed body; the what-if handler also Gets before body validation (stored start feeds the renewal.date check). **Schedule responses:** `next_renewal` is omitempty and appended LAST, present only when term metadata exists, computed from the generating spec (the what-if passes the composed scenario spec, so a simulated renewal moves it). **Live data:** the seeded mortgage (3e92806f-…) was PATCHed to term_years 5 + rate_type fixed via the API; NO rate events fabricated — the first renewal (2031) is in the future, so renewals are exercised through the what-if. **Live probe (post-rebuild):** GET amortization carried no `next_renewal` → after the PATCH it appended LAST = "2031-01-15" → what-if renewal 4.39 @ 2031-01-15 → schedule.next_renewal "2036-01-15" with a verbatim no-op (base 1326.18, payoff 2050-08-22 index 600, months_saved 0, interest_saved exactly "0.00" not "-0.00"). **Gates:** go build/vet/test all packages ok (17/17); 21/21 mortgage server tests incl. new TestMortgageAmortizationNextRenewal, TestMortgageWhatIfRenewal (same-rate no-op; 6.00 → interest_saved "-59219.99" with the payoff preserved), TestMortgageWhatIfPaymentFrequency (biweekly: idx 650, months_saved −350, interest_saved "963.73", base "1339.37", payoff 2050-12-01 — the 14-day grid doesn't land on the 15th, correcting the earlier probe note), TestMortgagesCreateIgnoresEvents. Frontend unchanged (round 24); no migration (0016+ still untouched).
- 2026-11-11: mortgage UI — term, rate type, rate-change events, renewal what-ifs (round 24) — frontend-only round 2 of the 23→24→25 mortgage sequence; surfaces the round-23 backend contract verbatim (NO backend change, no container rebuild — vite dev serves source). **API client (lib/api/mortgages.ts):** `MortgageRateEvent {effective_date, annual_rate, rate_type, action}`; `Mortgage` += `term_years`/`rate_type`/`rate_events` appended LAST (wire order); `MortgageCreate` += optional `term_years?`/`rate_type?` (client-sent `rate_events` stay ignored on create — events only via PATCH); `MortgagePatch` — all 11 fields optional, nil = untouched, non-nil `rate_events` = FULL-ARRAY REPLACEMENT (`[]` clears) — + `patchMortgage(id, body)`; `AmortizationSchedule` += `next_renewal?: string` (omitempty — present only when term metadata exists); `WhatIfRequest` += `payment_frequency?` (monthly/semi_monthly/biweekly) + `renewal? {date, annual_rate, rate_type?}`. **Page script:** `detailMortgage = $derived.by(selectedMortgage)` so the template null-narrows inside `{#if detailMortgage}` (the codebase idiom — `{@const}` must be an immediate block child and there is no `#each [x]` narrowing idiom); the amortization fetch was pulled into seq-guarded `loadAmortization()`, re-run after a successful rate-event PATCH because a renewal moves `next_renewal`; the empty-selection `$effect` now just bumps the seq to discard in-flight work. What-if state += `wiPayFreq`/`wiRenewDate`/`wiRenewRate`/`wiRenewType` + `wiFreqApplied` (true only when the LAST sent body carried `payment_frequency` — then `months_saved` is measured in payment PERIODS and the compare label switches to "Periods saved"); prefills mirror the stored spec (`wiPayFreq = m.payment_frequency`, `wiRenewDate = nextRenewalOf(m) ?? ''`, `wiRenewRate = m.annual_rate`, `wiRenewType` with legacy `''` reading as fixed — the client fabricates nothing, matching the backend); `compare()` sends `payment_frequency` ONLY when it differs from the stored frequency and `renewal` only when a date is set, always with an explicit `rate_type` (a cleared date = no simulated renewal). The add form gained `term_years` (0–40, step 1, placeholder "0 = no term") + `rate_type`, and `addMortgage` sanitizes with `Math.trunc(form.term_years ?? 0)` — an emptied number input binds `null` at runtime despite its number type. Rate-event section: `applyRateEvents(id, events)` = full-array `patchMortgage` → swap the row in the `mortgages` list via map → `loadAmortization()`; `addRateEvent()` appends then sorts ascending before sending (a duplicate date 400s server-side and surfaces VERBATIM — "rate event effective_dates must be strictly ascending"); `removeRateEvent(i)` filters by index. Helpers: `rateTypeLabel` (legacy `''` → "Fixed") and `nextRenewalOf` — a client mirror of `NextRenewalDate` (last re_amortize event date else start + term, UTC date math) used for INPUT PREFILL ONLY; the schedule's `next_renewal` is the display source of truth. **Template:** list-row subtitle reads `rate % Fixed/Variable / start`; the KPI line gains a fifth "Next renewal" card gated on `next_renewal !== undefined` (the grid class toggles `lg:grid-cols-5`/`-4` — both full literal class strings for tailwind); the add form gains the term + rate-type fields; a "Rate changes" panel (title/hint, empty state or `divide-y` rows — date, rate + type, action label, per-row Remove disabled while busy — plus a 5-column add form, server 400s verbatim) sits between the schedule table and the what-if panel, wrapped in `{#if detailMortgage}`; the what-if panel gains a payment-frequency select (monthly/semi_monthly/biweekly — DISTINCT from the existing `wiFreq`, which is the extra-payment frequency) + renewal date/rate/type; the compare table's months label is `wiFreqApplied ? cmp_periods : cmp_months` and a "Next renewal" row (base vs scenario, `?? '—'`) appears between months-saved and interest-saved when EITHER schedule carries one. **i18n:** 25 new keys in the `mortgage` section of en.json (the only locale); the rendered-numbers contract is preserved — every displayed amount is still the raw API string, and the only new displayed value is the raw `next_renewal` date. Legacy mortgages (no term metadata) render EXACTLY as before — no fifth card, no renewal row. **Gates:** svelte-check 0 errors / 0 warnings + vite build ok, both run inside the node:26.13.0 frontend dev container (no node on the host — `docker exec personal_finance_tracker-frontend-1 npm run check|build`; the `./frontend:/app` bind mount serves live source); dev-server smoke: `GET /mortgage` 200 (vite compiled the new template) and the live mortgage's amortization carries `next_renewal` "2031-01-15" over 600 payments, so the seeded 485k mortgage now shows the fifth KPI card.

- 2026-11-11: transaction matching — actuals vs plan, drift detection, rate-change suggestions (round 25) — final round of the 23→24→25 mortgage sequence; the user's requirement that "transactions are scanned to match payments to it to populate the amortization schedule (and update it if they pay off extra, or get a rate change and don't change their payments to compensate)". User decision (round 23): matching = **Recompute from actuals** — a read-only report; **nothing auto-mutates**. **No SQL migration** (0001–0015 untouched; the report is computed on demand from the spec blob + account transactions). **Endpoint:** `GET /api/v1/mortgages/{id}/matching` — no body, no mutation; route registered right after the what-if route in server.go. **Engine (internal/mortgage/matching.go, pure stdlib):** `Match(spec, txns)` — debits (Amount < 0) pair with the nearest plan date within a 72 h window; **multiple debits on one plan date SUM into that row's payment**; unpaired debits → off-grid `extras`; the actual balance mirrors the engine arithmetic in FULL precision (`bal = bal + bal·r_k − P_k`; round-2 only on record), `r_k` = last stored rate event with Date ≤ d_k (else the spec rate) — actuals thus reflect recorded rate events without re-derivation; payoff when `paid ≥ bal + accrued` (later debits → extras). **Classification:** `delta = P(d) − W(d)` (W = the row's FULL scheduled payment, recurring extra included); |delta| ≤ $1.00 = normal; a **sustained run** = ≥4 consecutive same-sign deviations with spread `maxD−minD ≤ 1.00 + (maxW−minW)` where **W has plannedExtra stripped** (the extra is due when a payment lands in a new calendar bucket, first payment included) and **P̄ is computed with plannedExtra stripped per row** — so a payer who keeps the extra while raising the base yields P̄ = new base. **Rate-change candidate:** bisection on A∈[0,100] (200 iters) solving `amortize(B*, PeriodicRate(A), N*) = P̄` (B* = balance before the run's first row, N* = PayoffIndex − i); suggested only when |A* − in-effect rate| ≥ 0.25 pp, else fall-through (positive → extras / negative → shorts); no in-range fit → flagged fall-through `payment_below_minimum_payoff` (f(0)) / `payment_above_maximum_rate` (f(100)); `no_transactions` when there are no debits. **Convention gotcha (verified against the live probe):** the app-wide periodic rate is Canadian semi-annual compounding `(1 + A/200)^(2/ppy) − 1` — NOT the simple `A/ppy`; a naive hand-calc reads ~0.06 pp low (live suggestion 5.42 vs naive 5.36 — 5.42 is the correct bisection root, independently confirmed). **Wire DTO (internal/server/mortgageMatching.go):** top-level key order `scheduled_payment, planned_payoff_date, planned_payoff_index, actual_payoff_date, last_matched_date, actual_balance, planned_balance, periods_matched, periods_missing, extras, shorts, suggestions, flags, periods`; row = `date, payment, extra, short, balance, planned_balance`; amount = `date, amount`; suggestion = `effective_date, implied_annual_rate, observed_payment, observed_periods`; top-level extras/shorts hold ONLY off-grid (debit order), post-payoff, and non-sustained fall-through — the periods table (rows 0..lastRow) sets extra/short on EVERY deviating row, run rows included; amounts are 2-decimal strings, slices never null (`[]`), `actual_payoff_date`/`last_matched_date` nullable; no-match → `planned_balance` = principal. **Handler ordering:** 503 (either store nil) → auth → malformed/unknown id 404 (even with a malformed body) → 500 backstop on spec decode; account transactions pulled paged (Limit 100 until a short page); amount parse failure = 500 backstop. **Frontend (mortgage page):** seq-guarded `loadMatching()` (matchSeq) after detail load and after any rate-event PATCH; `applySuggestion(s)` inserts `{effective_date, annual_rate: implied_annual_rate, rate_type: m.rate_type === 'variable' ? 'variable' : 'fixed', action: 're_amortize'}` → sort → round-23 `patchMortgage` → re-runs `loadAmortization() + loadMatching()` (reuses evBusy/evError — a suggestion is a rate-event add with its own label); panel between the events panel and the what-if comment: 6-cell summary `<dl>`, amber flag row, suggestion rows with an Add button, extras/shorts in two columns, periods table keyed by p.date with green/red extra/short chips; 22 new `match_*` i18n keys (en.json; all 108 keys used by the page verified present). **Gates:** 13 engine tests (pairing, same-date summing, plannedExtra-strip run classification, T3 drift→suggestion pinning implied 5.40 / delta 136.82 on a flat plan, bisection flag paths, normal no-ops) + 5 handler tests (auth, 404, DTO shape, empty `[]`, 503); svelte-check 0 errors / 0 warnings; vite build ok. **Live probe (dev DB):** 20 debits seeded into "Home Mortgage" — rows 0–9 EXACT plan payments, rows 10–19 a rate-hike drift with the payer keeping the 200 extra (base 1463.00; 1663.00 on the extra rows). The seed was first run flat, but on the live plan which carries the recurring extra, flat debits alternate delta sign → no sustained run; with plannedExtra stripped, "1463 + 200 on extra rows" is the realistic hike scenario. Result: `periods_matched 20 / periods_missing 0`, `last_matched_date 2026-10-27`, `actual_balance 472485.67` vs `planned_balance 473906.67` (~$1.4 k below plan = the run's excess principal; rows 0–9 drift ≤ 1 ¢ vs plan from per-period interest rounding), `extras`/`shorts`/`flags` all `[]`, exactly ONE suggestion `2026-06-14 → 5.42 %`, observed `1463.00` × 10 periods, table rows 10–19 extras +139.57…+142.34. **The suggestion was deliberately NOT applied live** — applying would mutate the demo spec and erase the interesting panel state; the apply path is already gated by round-23 PATCH tests.

- 2026-11-12: mortgage page money display — thousands separators + localised currency symbol, smooth chart line (round 26) — user feedback on the mortgage page: "graph is not a smooth line, it's a bunch of little stair-steps, that is ugly" and "dollar values don't have commas when over 3 digits and don't have dollar sign prefixes (remember to localise the dollar sign to the currency set for the mortgage account, don't assume they're always actual dollars)". **Chart fix:** `equitySeries` emitted `Math.round`ed integer coordinates into the 100×40 viewBox — with 600 points spanning ~485 000, integer snapping made every segment a horizontal stair; the x/balanceY/equityY now keep full float precision via `.toFixed(2)`, so the polyline is a smooth line (browser-verified: first 3 points `0.00,0.05 | 0.17,0.09 | 0.33,0.14`). **Money contract:** all displayed amounts go through `money(amount, currency)` / `moneyNum(value, currency)` — the backend's canonical 2-decimal strings are parsed EXACTLY to integer cents (`CENTS_RE` + `toCents`; unparseable input renders verbatim, never mangled; null/empty pass through as `''` so call-site sentinels like `|| '—'` stand) and formatted with `Intl.NumberFormat` in the BROWSER's own locale + the MORTGAGE ACCOUNT's currency (3-letter ISO-4217 from the accounts list). The currency SYMBOL IS NEVER ASSUMED — Intl supplies it (en-CA + CAD → "CA$485,000.00"); a missing or unknown currency code degrades to a plain grouped 2-decimal number with no symbol. Every displayed amount on the page is wrapped: list-row principal, the KPI line (base payment, total interest), year rows, expanded monthly rows, the matching panel (balances, observed payments, extras/shorts, the full periods table), what-if slider labels, and the compare table; rate/percent displays, counts, dates, and the API wire payloads (which stay canonical 2-decimal strings) are untouched. **Why the formatter cache is module-scope:** `money()` is called from template expressions, so `nfCache` cannot be component-state — a `SvelteMap` in instance scope mutated during render throws Svelte's `state_unsafe_mutation` and the component crashes into SvelteKit's `+error.svelte` "500 Internal Error" (indistinguishable at a glance from the old vite dev-500 — check the browser console for the Svelte error to tell them apart). The cache is memoisation, not state: it lives in the `<script module lang="ts">` block (note: Svelte 5.57 deprecates `context="module"` in favour of the `module` attribute). Even there, eslint `svelte/prefer-svelte-reactivity` fires on `new Map`/`new SvelteMap`/`new Date`, so the cache is a plain `Record<string, Intl.NumberFormat>` object — the render-safe escape hatch. **Account-currency lookup:** the list row passes `accountCurrency(m.account_id)` and the detail sections use `selectedCurrency = $derived` over `selectedMortgage()?.account_id` — the lookup key is the ACCOUNT id (comparing it against the mortgage id silently yields null and a symbol-less number). **Gates:** svelte-check 0/0, vite build green, eslint adds no new errors (the file's pre-existing `new Date` in `nextRenewalOf` remains); Playwright smoke against the live dev env renders "CA$485,000.00", "CA$1,326.18", "CA$292,642.33", with no raw unformatted amounts leaking and fractional polyline coordinates. Frontend-only; no backend change, no container rebuild.
- 2026-11-12: mortgage KPI card — "Payments" was a count, now labelled "Payment count" (round 27) — user feedback on the mortgage page: "the payments card in the mortgage view still lacks the currency prefix". **Diagnosis:** the card binds `amortization.payoff_index` — the NUMBER of payments to payoff (25 y semi-monthly = 600), not an amount. The 7.3 wire contract `scheduleOut` carries both: `payoff_index` (a count, `number`) and `total_paid` (sum of ALL payments, canonical 2-decimal string; live value 777642.33). A currency prefix on a count is wrong data, so the fix is disambiguation, not formatting. **Change:** i18n key `label_payments` → `label_payment_count` ("Payment count") in `frontend/src/lib/i18n/locales/en.json`; the KPI card call site in `frontend/src/routes/mortgage/+page.svelte` uses the new key with a "A COUNT, not an amount" comment; value rendering (`payoff_index`) untouched. **Gates:** svelte-check 0/0, eslint unchanged (only the pre-existing `new Date` error), vite build green; Playwright smoke renders "Payment count 600", the old capitalized "Payments" label is gone, and every round-26 CA$ needle still passes. Frontend-only; no backend change, no container rebuild. **Follow-up available:** if an AMOUNT is wanted in that slot, a "Total paid" KPI card is a one-line add — `money(amortization.total_paid, selectedCurrency)` (live CA$777,642.33).
- 2026-11-12: balance & equity chart — axis indices, hover crosshair, static today line (round 28) — user feedback on the mortgage page: "the balance and equity graph lacks indices, the ability to move the mouse along the axes and see the values at that point in the graph (should have dotted lines, going from each line to the x+y axes along with labels to show the values at those points, the points being the spots on each line at the x-axis position of the mouse). there should also be a statically-added vertical line+labels for the current date/most recent payment". **Rebuild:** the old 100×40 viewBox stretched with `preserveAspectRatio="none"` makes any SVG text into smeared blobs — the chart is now a uniform-scale **800×260** viewBox (`class="mt-2 w-full cursor-crosshair"`, no `preserveAspectRatio="none"`, no fixed height — the aspect ratio keeps text crisp at any width) with `PLOT = {x0:108, x1:788, y0:20, y1:224}` gutters for the axis labels. **Axes:** y ticks at 0/25/50/75/100% of principal in Intl **compact** notation in the ACCOUNT's currency (module-scope `compactNumberFormat` + `compactCache`; live: `CA$0 / CA$121.3K / CA$242.5K / CA$363.8K / CA$485K` — uppercase K in en-CA); x ticks at year boundaries from the first-payment year (step 5/2/1 by span) through the payoff year (live: 2026/2031/2036/2041/2046/2050). **Hover crosshair:** `chartMove` maps `clientX` through `getBoundingClientRect` into the viewBox and snaps to the NEAREST payment index (`let hoverI = $state<number | null>`, `let chartSvg = $state<SVGSVGElement | null>` — must be `let`, `bind:this` to a `const` fails svelte-check AND vite build); the snapped point draws halo dots on both series plus **dotted guides** (`stroke-dasharray="4 3"`) from each point to BOTH axes, masked value labels on the y-axis (balance + equity via `money`) and a masked date label on the x-axis, with a collision offset when `|by−ey| < 16`. **Today line:** static amber-500 vertical line + masked label at the current date, clamped into the schedule window; `todayISO()` is built with `Intl.DateTimeFormat.formatToParts` in module scope — no `new Date` anywhere (the eslint SvelteDate rule would flag it, and the module-scope memo keeps the render hot path allocation-free). **Data semantics (API-verified):** `payments[i].balance` is the **post-payment** balance — first point `108,20.27` (CA$484,352.16, NOT the full principal), last exactly `788,224` (CA$0.00); the invariant `by+ey = y0+y1 = 244` holds at every point (checked mid-flight: CA$282,267.06 + CA$202,732.94 = CA$485,000.00). **No new i18n keys** — tick/hover values are data, not UI strings. **Gates:** svelte-check 0/0, eslint unchanged (only the pre-existing `new Date` at L303), vite build green; full Playwright regression green — 12 static labels, 600 pts/series, interior points fractional, hover i=0 (`CA$484,352.16` / `CA$647.84` / `2026-01-15`, sub-$1,000 equity carries no thousands comma) and i=599 (`CA$0.00` / `CA$485,000.00` / `2050-08-22`), pointer-leave clears all four dots. **Test caveat (NOT an app bug):** headless Chromium at DPR 1 **floors mouse `clientX`/`clientY` to integer CSS px** — for CDP-dispatched (Playwright) AND synthetic `new MouseEvent()` events (verified: aim 1100.75 → page receives 1100) — so the 598/599 snap boundary at offset 935.076 px means a Playwright aim at the exact right edge lands on 598; edge tests must aim ≥1px INSIDE the svg's right edge (the page's own clamp absorbs overshoot). Frontend-only; no backend change, no container rebuild.

- 2026-11-12: balance & equity chart — "where am I right now" static labels (round 29) — user feedback on the mortgage page: "the cursor-based stuff looks great. for the static line, let's move the x-axis label to the bottom, and include y-axis labels for the balance+equity lines where it intersects as well, people will want that quick visual of 'where am I at right now'". **Date label relocation:** the masked label moves from above the plot (y0−17 / y0−6, 11px) to BELOW the axis, into the tick band (rect y = PLOT.y1+6, text baseline y = PLOT.y1+17, 12px/600, still amber-600/dark:amber-400) — drawn BEFORE the xTicks `{#each}` so a year tick re-renders over the white mask if it ends up beneath it; the text x is pushed a tick-width (50px) clear of any tick within 50px (`xt.x <= todayX ? xt.x+50 : xt.x−50`, single pass, then re-clamped to [x0+40, x1−40]) so it never sits on a year label (live: 2026 tick @108 → lx 158). **Y-axis crossing labels:** the DISPLAYED values are the real post-payment amounts of the most recent payment on or before today — `iNow` by ISO string compare (`s.payments[i].date <= todayISO()`), not the interpolated point (live iNow=20 = 2026-11-11 → CA$473,244.26 balance / CA$11,755.74 equity) — while the label ANCHOR is the today line's linear crossing between the two bracketing points (`fr = (todayX − pts[i0].x) / (pts[i0+1].x − pts[i0].x)`); the chart derived's `today` object now carries `{x, lx, label, iNow, balLy, eqLy}`. Label baselines mirror the hover's close-curve offsets (`close ? (balTop ? by−11 : by+5) : by+4`) PLUS a nudge the transient hover doesn't need: a STATIC label must not park over an axis index, so if `|ly − (tick.y+4)| < 13` it shifts 13px away (live: balLy 37 off the CA$485K baseline, eqLy 215 off the CA$0 baseline). Mask+text markup is identical to the hover labels (rect at x0−8−W, 12px/600, zinc-700/zinc-200, W = len×6.8+8), computed via `{@const}` as direct children of `{#if chart}`. **Gates:** svelte-check 0/0, eslint unchanged (only the pre-existing `new Date` error), vite build green; full Playwright regression green — every round-28 needle (axis ticks, polyline endpoints 108,20.27/788,224, hover i=0 → CA$484,352.16/CA$647.84/2026-01-15, hover i=599 → CA$0.00/CA$485,000.00/2050-08-22, leave clears) plus round-29: exactly one date label at x="158" y="241", y-labels CA$473,244.26 @ y="37" and CA$11,755.74 @ y="215", 3 static mask rects. Frontend-only; no backend change, no container rebuild.

- 2026-11-12: balance & equity chart — horizontal guides to the y-axis + value labels moved INSIDE the plot (round 30) — user feedback on the mortgage page: "there are no lines going horizontally from the intersection of the fixed line+the balance+equity lines over to the y-axis, which makes the new labels feel disjointed, those should exist / we should also move the balance labels into the graph as opposed to outside along the axis, as it can make the axis crowded if they are physically close to the normal labels". **Guides:** two dashed amber-500 horizontal lines (`stroke-dasharray="4 3"`, width 1) from the today line's crossings to the y-axis — `x1=PLOT.x0` → `x2=chart.today.x` at `y = balNow.y / eqNow.y` (the same interpolated crossings the labels anchor on) — drawn immediately after the vertical today line and BEFORE the polylines, so the curves render over the guide ends and the junction reads as "line meeting curve". **In-plot labels:** the balance/equity value labels move from the y-axis gutter into the plot, 8px RIGHT of the today line (`lx = todayX + 8`, `text-anchor="start"`, baseline `crossing + 4`), with the hover's close-curve offset retained (`|by−ey| < 16` → top label `y−11`, bottom `y+5`) so they never collide where the curves converge; the round-29 tick nudge is REMOVED — it only existed to dodge axis indices, and inside the plot there are none to dodge. Near the right edge the label flips to the LEFT of the line (`todayX + 8 + w > PLOT.x1` → `lx = todayX − 8`, `anchor="end"`) so it can't leave the plot as the today line approaches payoff. **Geometry lives in the derived:** all label strings, widths, and positions are computed in the chart `$derived.by` via a `nowLabel(lbl, y, onTop)` helper returning `{lbl, y, ly, lx, w, anchor, rx}` (mask x = `lx−4`, i.e. `lx−w+4` when flipped; W = len×6.8+8 unchanged), so the template renders `chart.today.balNow` / `chart.today.eqNow` attribute-by-attribute — the round-29 `{@const}` block (tp / balNowLbl / eqNowLbl / balNowW / eqNowW) is deleted and no template-side string math remains. Mask+text pairs draw AFTER both polylines (white/zinc-900 rect, 14px tall, rx=2) so the curves pass under the text. **Gates:** svelte-check 0/0, eslint unchanged (only the pre-existing `new Date`), vite build green; Playwright regression green — every round-28 needle unchanged (ticks, endpoints 108,20.27/788,224, hover i=0 / i=599 labels, leave clears) plus round-30: date label still x="158" y="241"; in-plot labels CA$473,244.26 @ x="138.78" y="28.95" and CA$11,755.74 @ x="138.78" y="223.05", both anchor=start; exactly 2 amber dashed guides (108→130.78) at y="24.95" and y="219.05"; label masks at (134.78, 17.95, w96.4) and (134.78, 212.05, w89.6); 3 static mask rects. Frontend-only; no backend change, no container rebuild.
- 2026-11-12: what-if scenario overlaid on the balance & equity chart (round 31) — user feedback on the mortgage page: "the what-if tools should also render new graphs so users have the visualisation of old vs new instead of just a table". **Overlay:** while a comparison is active the what-if schedule draws on the EXISTING chart as two DASHED polylines (`stroke-dasharray="5 4"`, width 1.5) in the same colors as the base curves (zinc balance, green equity) so the stored curves stay primary; the scenario's payments are mapped BY DATE (not index) onto the shared time axis — each payment's x is `px((t(date) − t0)/span)`, y the same `py(balance/principal)` / `py(1 − balance/principal)` as the stored curves since both schedules share the principal — and it ends in a payoff dot (sky-500 r=3.5 over a white halo r=5.5) at its last payment. **Shared domain:** the time axis widens only if the scenario payoff is LATER than the stored one: `tEnd = max(t1, t(scenDate))` with the stored curves rescaled into the wider window by `storedFrac = (t1−t0)/span` (stored point x, the "today" interpolation `fNow` and the hover crosshair snap are all multiplied by it). `storedFrac = 1` — the common case and, under the current what-if contract, the only reachable one (the engine re-amortizes over the fixed amortization horizon and extra principal only shortens the schedule, so a scenario payoff is never later) — keeps every stored coordinate byte-identical to the plain chart, leaving the round-28/30 regression needles untouched; the x-axis year ticks compute over the widened domain (endYear from the scenario payoff when later). **Template & legend:** the scenario block renders between the two stored polylines and the "now" label masks (masks stay on top); a legend span "Scenario (dashed)" (dashed border swatch) shows only while a scenario is active (`chart_legend_scenario` in en.json); the overlay auto-clears through `resetWhatIf()`, which already runs on reset, selection change and schedule reload. **Gates:** svelte-check 0/0, eslint unchanged (5 pre-existing repo errors, none new), vite build green; Playwright regression green — all round-28/30 needles unchanged plus round-31: with an extra-500/mo compare, exactly 2 dashed polylines, scenario balance `108,20.4 → 782.32,224` / equity `108,223.6 → 782.32,20`, payoff dot at 782.32/224, stored endpoints unchanged at `108,20.27 / 788,224` (storedFrac=1), legend present, the 2050 tick unchanged, the compare table shows the scenario payoff 2050-06-08, and Reset returns the dashed count, legend and dot to zero. Frontend-only; no backend change, no container rebuild.
- 2026-11-13: manual classification of aberrant mortgage payments — spec-blob answers, lazy single notifications, dual-path answering (round 32) — user ask on the mortgage feature: "people need a way to manually classify abberant transactions (not just the amount in the schedule) as one-off payments against principal or a rate change. these should always be flagged in notifications and when navigating to the mortgage tab, should be a modal pop-up forcing the user to answer the question before proceeding to ensure data is accurate". **No SQL migration** (0001–0015 untouched) — the answer lives in the existing DEK-encrypted spec blob. **Spec model (internal/repo/mortgages.go):** `MortgageSpec` += `PaymentClassifications []PaymentClassification` appended LAST (pre-existing sealed blobs unmarshal to nil); `PaymentClassification = {Date, Kind, Classification, AnnualRate *string, RateType *string, Action *string}` — Kind `"extra"|"short"|"suggestion"` (Date = the debit date, or the run's effective date for a suggestion), Classification `"one_off"|"rate_change"|"rejected"`, and the three rate fields set ONLY for rate_change (nil otherwise — canonical 2-decimal rate, fixed/variable type, re_amortize/keep_payment action). `MortgagePatch.PaymentClassifications` is a FULL-ARRAY replacement (nil = untouched, non-nil = replace), compared in the changed-check exactly like RateEvents. **Engine — two behavior changes this round:** (a) a `re_amortize` renewal now re-amortizes over the remaining original amortization INCLUDING the event's own period — `pay = amortize(bal, r, n−k+1)` (was `n−k`) — so the event's own payment is already the new re-amortized amount, the payoff date is preserved, and the matching solver solves exactly this model (pin: the 6.00 renewal's interest_saved moved −59219.99 → −59046.33); (b) a sustained run whose implied rate is within `matchMinShift` (0.25 pp) of the in-effect rate is the plan's own re-amortization drift, not an anomaly, and is now ABSORBED — its rows' `delta` is nilled so the periods report as normal (no extra/short badges or amounts) instead of falling through to extras/shorts as before (internal/mortgage/matching.go). **Endpoint (internal/server/mortgageClassify.go):** `POST /api/v1/mortgages/{id}/classifications`, body `{date, kind, classification, annual_rate?, rate_type?, action?}` — plain strings, all validated by `classifyProblems` (strict YYYY-MM-DD; kind and classification closed enums — "classification must be one_off, rate_change or rejected"; a rate_change additionally requires a positive canonical rate, a fixed/variable type and a re_amortize/keep_payment action) joined with "; " into a single 400; the rate-event upsert is gated on `rate_change`, so `one_off` and `rejected` store the answer with nil rate fields and write NO rate event; the (kind, date) pair must exist in the CURRENT matching report or it 400s ("no {kind} on {date} in the current matching report"); the matching notification for that item is marked read best-effort. **Lazy notifications (internal/server/mortgageMatching.go):** `ensureAberrantNotifications` runs ONLY on `GET /matching` — it creates ONE `mortgage_aberrant` notification per still-unclassified item (extra/short/suggestion), deduped by `FindByKindRef` on (kind, ref) in ANY read state so revisits never duplicate; ref = `{mortgage_id, kind, date}`; Find/Create failures are logged only — the report still serves. `annotateMatchingOut` stamps the stored classification onto every extra/short/suggestion in the report, so an answered item shows its badge and a `rejected` suggestion — the plan still drifts, so the matcher re-derives it — comes back ANNOTATED with the stored answer (the dedup means no new notification) until it is re-classified. **Dual-path answering:** (1) MORTGAGE TAB — a full-screen blocking modal (Escape must NOT close it) open exactly while the selected mortgage has ≥1 item with `classification === null` (a `pendingItems` derived over extras/shorts/suggestions); each item's form defaults to one_off (extras/shorts) or rate_change (suggestions); answering POSTs /classifications, swaps the updated mortgage, stamps the badge (one_off / rate_change / rejected), and re-runs the amortization load when a rate_change lands. (2) NOTIFICATIONS PAGE — a suggestion ref (kind "suggestion") gets an inline action panel instead of the deep link: it lazy-fetches on open (GET /matching to find the suggestion by effective_date + GET /mortgages/{id} for the mortgage's rate_type) and offers Accept (the implied rate + the mortgage's rate_type + re_amortize), a Tweak form pre-filled with those values (local non-empty-rate validation; an API 400 surfaced inline), or Reject (classification "rejected", no rate fields); success drops the panel and refetches the list so the row flips to read; extra/short refs keep the existing "Go to mortgage" deep link. **`rejected` semantics:** a durable answer — stored, no rate event, its notification marked read, it stops forcing the modal, and it stays re-classifiable (a later rate_change replaces it in place and the re-amortized plan then explains the payments). **Dead code:** the round-23 "Apply suggestion" button on the mortgage page is now unreachable (the modal covers the page while any item is unclassified) and is removed — the button, its `applySuggestion` handler, its import, and the 2 i18n keys `mortgage.match_suggestion_apply` / `match_suggestion_applying` (the `match_suggestion_observed`/`periods` keys stay; they serve the matching panel). **i18n (en.json):** +17 `notifications.suggestion_*` keys (review / loading / gone / implied / current_type / accept / accepting / tweak / tweak_close / reject / rejecting / rate_label / rate_type_label / action_label / apply / applying / rate_required) + the `mortgage.classify_*` modal keys. **API client (lib/api/mortgages.ts):** +`getMortgage(id)`; the `classifyPayment` request type widened to `'one_off'|'rate_change'|'rejected'`. **Gates:** go build + vet clean, gofmt clean, and the FULL suite green (all packages ok — incl. internal/mortgage with the changed renewal pin and the new `TestMortgageClassifyRejected`, which GETs first then pins no rate event, the stored nil-rate-fields shape, mark-read, the annotated re-derivation, and an in-place re-classify to rate_change; 5/5 classify tests pass); svelte-check 0 errors / 0 warnings, eslint exactly the 5 pre-existing repo errors (none new), vite build green. **E2E (Playwright):** first run — the full round-28/30/31 regression (chart geometry, what-if overlay) green WITH the new blocking modal up on the mortgage page (hover via synthetic mousemove on the svg; Compare/Reset via el.click() in evaluate past the z-50 overlay; Escape and backdrop click both no-ops), the A-mortgage modal flow green (exactly the extra card, one_off default, no rate input, answer closes the modal, "one-off principal" badge), console clean (signed-out fresh-load 401s + favicon 404 only) — but the notifications suggestion panel got stuck on "Loading suggestion…" forever. **App bug (code-verified; backend ruled out — the same endpoints answer curl in ms):** `openPanel` stored a RAW panel object into the `$state` record and then mutated it through the local raw reference (`panel.loading = false` in finally), bypassing the $state proxy so no invalidation ever fired. **Fix (notifications/+page.svelte):** re-bind after storing (`panel = panels[n.id]`) and mutate through the stored proxy; gates re-run with identical results (0/0, the 5 pre-existing eslint errors, build green). Targeted re-run after the fix — every part green: the panel now loads (prefill 5.42 / Fixed, Accept/Tweak/Reject rendered) → Reject (row flips read; the suggestion re-derives ANNOTATED "rejected"; no duplicate notification — the (kind, ref) dedup holds) → re-open the now-READ row → Tweak → Apply 5.42 (B gains rate event {2026-06-14, 5.42, fixed, re_amortize} layered on the stored 4.39 spec; suggestions go empty and the 10-period run is now EXPLAINED — planned_balance 473906.67 → 474527.02, actual−planned gap ~1421 → 21.89, periods_matched 20; NOTE the matching `scheduled_payment` is BY DESIGN invariant at 1326.18 — it is the stored plan's BASE payment, captured before rate events and pinned by four tests — the plan's movement shows in planned_balance and the explained run) → then NO blocking modal on either mortgage (A's extra badge; B's suggestion row gone as explained, extra badge, rate event rendered in the details) → the "Go to mortgage" deep link → console baseline clean; end state: both extras `one_off`, all 3 `mortgage_aberrant` notifications read, no pending items anywhere. **Logged cosmetic gaps (pre-existing, not fixed):** the two extra-notification rows are textually identical (no mortgage identity in the row text — the suggestion row is unique: "A sustained payment change since {date} needs classification"), and "Go to mortgage" is a bare `/mortgage` link (no id param) that always lands on the default mortgage.

- 2026-11-15: actionable notifications — in-place confirm/reject/edit, `?m=` deep link, self-healing list (round 33) — user feedback on the notifications: "i have notifications that say transactions need review, in both mortgages and just for classification. however, nothing seems to be up-to-date, as i can't actually click on any of them to 'do anything'. the classification ones just aren't clickable at all. the mortgage ones — are only clickable with 'go to mortgage', when the entire card should be clickable with a modal to do the confirm/reject/edit right there in-place — when i click 'go to mortgage', i just... go there. there's no action to take at at all. i think we need to make sure that only still-relevant notifications are kept there". **Actionable cards (frontend, `routes/notifications/+page.svelte`):** `isActionable(n)` = kind `review` (always) or `mortgage_aberrant` with a parseable ref (`parseAberrantRef` → `{mortgage_id, kind, date}`); for actionable cards the card's content `div` — NOT the outer `<li>`, which still hosts the sibling "Mark read" button — renders `role="button"` + `tabindex=0` + `aria-label` = `notifications.card_aria` ("Open to take action") + a hover border, and click or Enter opens an **in-place** modal — no navigation at all. **In-place review modal:** fetches the LIVE queue (`GET /v1/review`) on open and keeps every row's state (category pick, in-flight action, inline editor) across re-fetches; per row: Accept (`POST /v1/review/{id}/accept`), the new **Reject** (`POST /v1/review/{id}/reject`, no body — non-destructive: the transaction stays uncategorized but `review_pending = false`, so the row leaves the queue), Reprompt, and an inline editor that saves via the new `PATCH /v1/transactions/{id}` (`patchTransaction` in `api/transactions.ts`); plus accept-all-for-a-category (`POST /v1/review/accept-all`). **In-place aberrant modal:** answers the referenced item over the round-32 `POST /v1/mortgages/{id}/classifications` — `one_off`, `rate_change` (suggestions prefill the implied rate; "Tweak" opens the rate form), or the new **`rejected`** (the item counts as answered; `mortgageClassify.go`'s validator now accepts `one_off|rate_change|rejected` and the spec's `PaymentClassification` row stores `classification:"rejected"` with no rate fields). **Backend — classify deletes its own notification** (`notifications.Delete` after a successful classify; `ErrNotificationNotFound` is tolerated — the row may already be gone via the self-heal). **Backend — list self-heal (`GET /v1/notifications`; `notifications.go` + `mortgageMatching.go`):** the list endpoint runs the self-heals FIRST so it never serves a stale row: `syncReviewNotification` reconciles the review rows to the live queue (the count is authoritative — zero pending → EVERY `review` row purged; N>0 → exactly one row kept (the newest), refreshed to the live count + ref, duplicates purged; none → one created), and `syncAberrantNotifications` is **delete-only** (creation stays on `GET /matching`'s lazy path with its dedup): rows whose ref does not parse are deleted outright, rows whose mortgage is gone are deleted, and rows whose `(mortgage, kind, date)` item is no longer unclassified (answered, no longer reported by the engine, or the mortgage was edited) are deleted; every sync failure is log-only — a notification write must never change the response of the mutation that triggered it. **Mortgage page — `?m=` deep link (`routes/mortgage/+page.svelte`):** the aberrant modal's "Go to mortgage" footer is now `a[href="/mortgage?m={id}"]`; the page picks `?m=` up on the initial load in `loadMortgages`, and a write effect (`selectedId` → `goto(resolve('/mortgage?m=' + id), {replaceState:true})`) keeps the URL in step with the mortgage selector afterwards. **Deliberately NO read-back effect:** right after a selector click the param still names the PREVIOUS mortgage, and a read effect firing in the same Svelte flush silently reverts the click (found in E2E — the click "succeeded" but the URL and `aria-pressed` both reverted); back/forward needs none either since the write uses `replaceState`, so the app never pushes its own `?m=` history entries. Landing on an aberrant deep link opens the round-32 **blocking** classify modal — that modal IS the "action to take", so the page legitimately waits (the selector sits under its backdrop) until the items are answered. **Badge (`AppShell.svelte`):** the unread-count badge keeps its 60 s interval refresh and now also listens for `ftrk:unread-changed` — a `CustomEvent` the notifications page dispatches after every in-place answer (accept / reject / edit / classify / mark-read) — so the badge updates immediately. **Gates:** `go test` green (server, repo, categorize); svelte-check 0/0; eslint 5 errors = pre-existing HEAD baseline in untouched files; prettier check 20 files = pre-existing drift, zero new; vite build green. **E2E:** 12-step Playwright run (in-place modals, deep link, selector↔URL binding on a clean baseline, hygiene) fully green; its (i) restores the clean baseline at the end.
- 2026-11-20: savings / registered plans — contributions vs limits + appreciation over time (rounds 50–55) — user scope: "we don't need to provide features like tracking security values, just the account balances, contributions vs limits, appreciation over time, and contributions+withdrawal transactions". **Backend (rounds 50–52, `0019_savings.sql`):** the six plan account types (RESP/RDSP/RRSP/TFSA/FHSA/RRIF) with beneficiary name + encrypted-only birth year, manual balance snapshots, per-type/per-year limit overrides, manual room adjustments; `internal/plans` embeds the statutory figures (canada.ca, as at 2026 — TFSA $7,000, RRSP $33,810, FHSA $8,000/yr + $40,000 lifetime, RESP $50,000 lifetime/beneficiary, RDSP $200,000 lifetime/beneficiary, RRIF minimum-withdrawal factors); the display-only room engine (counted contributions = positive rows with `external_origin` NULL/'employer'/'other' — government_grant/inherited never count; no automatic historical accumulation, `plan_room_adjustments` is the manual mechanism). Endpoints: `GET /v1/savings/room`, `PATCH /v1/me/birth-year`, `GET/PUT/DELETE /v1/savings/limits`, `GET/POST/DELETE /v1/savings/adjustments`, `GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE /v1/savings/snapshots`, `GET /v1/savings/equity` (snapshots + opening balance + transactions between snapshots). **Frontend (rounds 53a–54):** the `/savings` room page, the `/savings/[accountId]` appreciation page, plan fields in the settings account forms, transfer-from + external-origin in manual entry, the `savings` dashboard widget kind (`frontend/src/lib/dashboards/SavingsWidget.svelte`). **Gates:** go vet + go test green (rounds 50–52); svelte-check 0/0, en.json prettier-clean (rounds 53a–54). D-032 added.
- 2026-11-25: Phase 10 §10.1 — open-source release + artifact wiring (round 58) — added `LICENSE.md` (PolyForm Noncommercial 1.0.0), the public `upstream` remote (`tarfeef101/stonks`), and the Makefile GHCR wiring: `REGISTRY`/`IMAGE_TAG` (short SHA), `images`/`images-push` retargeted to `ghcr.io/tarfeef101/stonks-{backend,frontend}`, a new `images-scan` (trivy HIGH/CRITICAL, `--exit-code 1`, docker-socket mount), and a pinned **helm 4.3.1** container recipe — no official helm image exists, so `alpine:3.22` + the get.helm.sh tarball, sha256-verified on every run (`HELM_SHA256`); targets `helm-version` (smoke gate) / `helm-lint` / `helm-template` / `chart-push` (the latter three become runnable when the chart lands in §10.2). **Pin correction:** `aquasecurity/trivy` no longer exists on Docker Hub (the repo 404s — the official image moved to GHCR, and its tags carry no `v` prefix) → pinned `ghcr.io/aquasecurity/trivy:0.76.2` (latest, verified 2026-11-25 via the GHCR tag list). D-033 + D-034 added; stale `PUBLIC_AUTH0_*` → `PUBLIC_OIDC_*` in DEVELOPMENT.md + BLOCKERS.md (the D-030 rename). First `images-scan` gate run (round 58) found HIGH/CRITICAL findings in both `:2b8e75d` images, fixed by: `backend/go.mod` golang.org/x/net v0.61.0 → v0.62.0 (CVE-2026-78661, x/net/html DoS), goose CLI v3.29.0 → v3.30.2 in `backend/Dockerfile` (first release pinning x/net ≥ v0.62.0), and `RUN apk upgrade --no-cache` after `FROM nginx:alpine` in `frontend/Dockerfile` (brotli/curl/libexpat/libxml2/libxslt — all fixed in alpine 3.24).
